## Section: seg_85

#### LO 1: Define the normal distribution as a continuous model with parameters μ and σ and notation X ~ N(μ, σ).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Normal Distribution): Fill in the blank to complete the definition: A normal distribution is a continuous random variable with parameters μ (mean) and σ (standard deviation), denoted ____.
**Answer**: X ~ N(μ, σ)

#### LO 2: Calculate probabilities or percentiles for X ~ N(μ, σ) using the given mean and standard deviation.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Normal Distribution): For X ~ N(10, 2), P(8 < X < 12) is best described as:
A. The area under the normal curve between x = 8 and x = 12 with μ = 10 and σ = 2.
B. The area under the standard normal curve to the right of z = 8.
C. The value of the pdf at x = 10.
D. The entire area under the normal curve.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: Interpret μ and σ as the center and spread of a bell-shaped curve and interpret areas under the curve as probabilities.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Normal Distribution): For X ~ N(50, 10), which statement is correct?
A. μ = 50 is the mean (center) and σ = 10 measures spread; the area under the curve over an interval gives the probability X is in that interval.
B. μ = 50 is the median and σ = 10 is the range; the curve's height gives probability.
C. μ = 50 is the mode and σ = 10 is the variance; probabilities are counts.
D. μ = 50 and σ = 10 are quartiles; areas under the curve are percentiles only, not probabilities.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 4: Define the standard normal distribution as Z ~ N(0, 1).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Standard Normal Distribution): Fill in the blank: The standard normal distribution is denoted ____.
**Answer**: Z ~ N(0, 1)

#### LO 5: Calculate areas and percentiles on the standard normal curve using z-values.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Standard Normal Distribution): Which of the following correctly defines the 90th percentile on the standard normal distribution?
A. Find z such that P(Z < z) = 0.90.
B. Find z such that P(Z > z) = 0.90.
C. Find x such that P(X < x) = 0.90 for any normal X with μ and σ.
D. Find z such that P(Z < z) = 0.10.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: Interpret locations on the standard normal scale in terms of standard deviations from the mean.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Standard Normal Distribution): On the standard normal scale, a z-value of −2 indicates that a value is:
A. 2 standard deviations below the mean.
B. 2 units below 0 on the x-axis but not related to standard deviations.
C. 2% below the mean.
D. Equal to the mean.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 7: Calculate a z-score using z = (x − μ) / σ for a normally distributed variable.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Z-Score): For X ~ N(20, 4), what is the z-score of x = 28?
A. 2
B. −2
C. 0.5
D. 4
**Answer**: A

#### LO 8: Interpret a z-score as how many standard deviations a value is above or below the mean.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Z-Score): A z-score of 1.5 means the observed value is:
A. 1.5 standard deviations above the mean.
B. 1.5 units above the mean regardless of σ.
C. 1.5 percent above the mean.
D. 1.5 standard deviations below the mean.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 9: Define a z-score as the linear transformation that standardizes X ~ N(μ, σ) to Z ~ N(0, 1).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Z-Score): Fill in the blank: The z-score is the linear transformation ____ that standardizes X ~ N(μ, σ) to Z ~ N(0, 1).
**Answer**: z = (x − μ)/σ

#### LO 10: Define the interquartile range (IQR) as Q3 − Q1.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Interquartile Range (IQR)): Fill in the blank: The interquartile range (IQR) equals ____.
**Answer**: Q3 − Q1

#### LO 11: Calculate the IQR from ordered data or from a normal model using Q1 and Q3.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Interquartile Range (IQR)): If Q1 = 18 and Q3 = 26 for a dataset, what is the IQR?
A. 8
B. 44
C. 4
D. 24
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: State that approximately 68%, 95%, and 99.7% of values lie within 1σ, 2σ, and 3σ of μ in a normal distribution.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Empirical Rule (68-95-99.7)): Fill in the blank: In a normal distribution, approximately ____ of values lie within 1σ, 2σ, and 3σ of the mean, respectively.
**Answer**: 68%, 95%, and 99.7%

#### LO 13: Calculate approximate proportions or x-ranges using the 68–95–99.7 rule for a normal distribution.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Empirical Rule (68-95-99.7)): For X ~ N(100, 10), approximately what percent of observations lie between 90 and 110?
A. 68%
B. 50%
C. 95%
D. 99.7%
**Answer**: A

#### LO 14: Calculate a kth percentile using k = μ + zσ or with invNorm given the area to the left.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Kth Percentile In A Normal Distribution): Fill in the blank: For X ~ N(μ, σ), the kth percentile corresponding to z is given by ____.
**Answer**: k = μ + zσ

#### LO 15: Explain the meaning of a percentile (e.g., 85th) as the value below which that percent of observations fall.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Kth Percentile In A Normal Distribution): In a normal model, the 85th percentile is best described as:
A. The value below which 85% of observations fall.
B. The z-score equal to 0.85.
C. The value above which 85% of observations fall.
D. The mean plus 0.85 standard deviations.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 16: Use normalcdf(lower, upper, mean, standard deviation) to compute normal probabilities such as P(a < X < b).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Normalcdf Function): Which calculator input computes P(45 < X < 60) for X ~ N(50, 5)?
A. normalcdf(45, 60, 50, 5)
B. normalcdf(50, 5, 45, 60)
C. normalcdf(45, 60, 0, 1)
D. invNorm(0.95, 50, 5)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 17: Define normalcdf as the calculator function that returns the probability for a normal variable between specified bounds given μ and σ.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Normalcdf Function): Fill in the blank: Calculator function for normal probabilities: ____.
**Answer**: normalcdf(lower x, upper x, mean, standard deviation)

#### LO 18: Use invNorm(area to the left, mean, standard deviation) to find normal percentiles and cutoffs.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: InvNorm Function): Which input finds k such that P(X < k) = 0.90 for X ~ N(100, 15)?
A. invNorm(0.90, 100, 15)
B. invNorm(0.10, 100, 15)
C. normalcdf(0.90, 100, 15, k)
D. invNorm(k, 100, 15)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 19: State that invNorm requires the area to the left, mean, and standard deviation and returns the value k such that P(X < k) equals the specified area.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: InvNorm Function): Fill in the blank: Calculator function for normal percentiles: ____.
**Answer**: invNorm(area to the left, mean, standard deviation)

## Section: seg_89

#### LO 1: Calculate approximate probabilities for sample means using the normal model with mean μ and standard error σ/√n as n increases.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sample Means): To approximate P(X̄ > 52) when μ = 50, σ = 10, and n = 25, which normal model should you use?
A. Normal with mean 50 and standard error 2
B. Normal with mean 50 and standard deviation 10
C. Normal with mean 52 and standard error 2
D. Normal with mean 0 and standard error 10/25
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: Interpret the central limit theorem as stating that, as n increases, the distribution of X̄ approaches normal with mean μ and variance σ²/n.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sample Means): As n increases, the distribution of X̄ approaches ______ with mean μ and variance σ²/n.
**Answer**: normal

#### LO 3: Define the sampling distribution of the mean as the distribution of X̄ formed from all samples of size n.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sampling Distribution Of The Mean): The sampling distribution of the mean is the distribution of ______ formed from sample means.
**Answer**: X̄

#### LO 4: Calculate the mean μ and standard error σ/√n of the sampling distribution given μ, σ, and n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sampling Distribution Of The Mean): If μ = 80, σ = 12, and n = 36, what are the mean and standard error of X̄?
A. Mean 80; standard error 2
B. Mean 80; standard error 12
C. Mean 80; variance 4
D. Mean 12; standard error 80
**Answer**: A

#### LO 5: Interpret the sampling distribution as approximately normal for large n, centered at μ with spread σ/√n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sampling Distribution Of The Mean): Which best describes the sampling distribution of X̄ for large n?
A. Approximately normal, centered at μ, with spread σ/√n
B. Approximately normal, centered at μ, with spread σ
C. Skewed right, centered at μ, with spread σ/√n
D. Exactly normal for any n, with spread σ²/n
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: State that the expected value of X̄ equals the population mean μ.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Mean Of The Sampling Distribution): The expected value of X̄ equals the ______.
**Answer**: population mean μ

#### LO 7: Calculate the expected value of X̄ from a given population mean μ.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Mean Of The Sampling Distribution): If the population mean is μ = 30, what is E[X̄]?
A. 30
B. 30/√n
C. σ
D. n
**Answer**: A

#### LO 8: Describe how the sample mean targets the population mean so that μ is the average of both X and X̄.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Mean Of The Sampling Distribution): Which statement best expresses how the sample mean targets the population mean?
A. The sample mean targets the population mean because μ is the average of both X and X̄.
B. The sample mean targets μ only when n = 1.
C. The sample mean targets μ because Var(X̄) = σ².
D. The sample mean targets the median of X.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 9: State that the variance of X̄ equals the population variance divided by the sample size n.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Variance Of The Sampling Distribution): Var(X̄) equals ______.
**Answer**: σ²/n

#### LO 10: Calculate Var(X̄) as σ²/n for a given population variance σ² and sample size n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Variance Of The Sampling Distribution): If σ² = 25 and n = 100, what is Var(X̄)?
A. 0.25
B. 2.5
C. 25
D. 0.05
**Answer**: A

#### LO 11: Define the standard error of the mean as the standard deviation of X̄, σ/√n.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Standard Error Of The Mean): The standard error of the mean is the standard deviation of X̄; ______.
**Answer**: σ/√n

#### LO 12: Calculate the standard error of the mean as σ/√n from a given σ and n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Standard Error Of The Mean): If σ = 15 and n = 25, what is the standard error of X̄?
A. 3
B. 15
C. 0.6
D. 5
**Answer**: A

#### LO 13: Describe the standard error as the typical distance between the sample mean and the population mean across repeated samples of size n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Standard Error Of The Mean): Which best describes the standard error of the mean?
A. It describes how far, on average, the sample mean is from the population mean in repeated samples of size n.
B. It equals σ²/n and measures variance.
C. It is the population standard deviation σ.
D. It is the standard deviation of individual observations.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 14: Define the z-score for a sample mean as z = (x̄ − μ)/(σ/√n).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Z-Score For Sample Means): The z-score for a sample mean is z = (x̄ − μ) / ______.
**Answer**: σ/√n

#### LO 15: Calculate the z-score for a sample mean using z = (x̄ − μ)/(σ/√n).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Z-Score For Sample Means): For μ = 100, σ = 20, n = 16, and x̄ = 105, what is z?
A. 0.25
B. 1
C. 4
D. 5
**Answer**: B

#### LO 16: Differentiate between the z-score for a sample mean, which uses σ/√n, and the z-score for an individual observation, which uses σ.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Z-Score For Sample Means): Which statement is correct about z-scores?
A. For a sample mean, divide by σ/√n; for an individual observation, divide by σ.
B. For a sample mean, divide by σ; for an individual observation, divide by σ/√n.
C. Both use σ/√n.
D. Both use σ.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 17: Define n as the number of values averaged in each sample.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sample Size Interpretation (n)): In this context, n is the ______.
**Answer**: number of values averaged in each sample

#### LO 18: Differentiate between n (the number of values in each sample) and the number of times the experiment is performed.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sample Size Interpretation (n)): An experiment is repeated 50 times, each time averaging 4 measurements to form one X̄. What is n?
A. 4
B. 50
C. 54
D. 200
**Answer**: A

#### LO 19: Use n correctly when computing the standard error σ/√n and when entering parameters for calculator functions involving sample means.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sample Size Interpretation (n)): You will collect 10 samples, each of size n = 36. Which value should be used in σ/√n when computing the standard error for X̄?
A. 36
B. 10
C. 360
D. 1
**Answer**: A

#### LO 20: Use normalcdf(lower, upper, mean, standard error of the mean) to find probabilities for sample means.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Normalcdf For Sample Mean Probabilities): For μ = 70, σ = 12, n = 9, to find P(X̄ ≤ 75), which calculator input is correct?
A. normalcdf(-1E99, 75, 70, 12/3)
B. normalcdf(-1E99, 75, 70, 12)
C. normalcdf(-1E99, 75, 0, 12/3)
D. normalcdf(70, 75, 70, 12/3)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 21: Identify the correct inputs for normalcdf with sample means: lower, upper, μ of the original distribution, and σ/√n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Normalcdf For Sample Mean Probabilities): Which set of inputs correctly matches normalcdf for sample means?
A. lower, upper, μ, σ/√n
B. lower, upper, μ, σ
C. lower, upper, x̄, σ/√n
D. lower, upper, 0, 1
**Answer**: A

#### LO 22: Use invNorm(area to the left, mean, standard error of the mean) to find percentiles of the sampling distribution of X̄.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: InvNorm For Sample Mean Percentiles): For μ = 100, σ = 30, n = 25, which input finds the 90th percentile of X̄?
A. invNorm(0.90, 100, 30/5)
B. invNorm(0.90, 100, 30)
C. invNorm(0.10, 100, 30/5)
D. invNorm(0.90, 0, 1)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 23: Interpret a percentile of the sampling distribution in context to explain what proportion of sample means fall below that value.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: InvNorm For Sample Mean Percentiles): If the 90th percentile of X̄ is 82, what does this mean?
A. 90% of sample means fall below 82.
B. 90% of individual observations fall below 82.
C. The probability the population mean is below 82 is 0.9.
D. 82 is 90% of μ.
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_91

#### LO 1: calculate the mean nμX and standard deviation σX√n of the normal model for ΣX under the central limit theorem for sums.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sums): Given μX = 4, σX = 3, and n = 16, what are the mean and standard deviation for the normal model of ΣX?
A. mean = 64, SD = 12
B. mean = 4, SD = 3
C. mean = 64, SD = 3
D. mean = 4, SD = 12
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: interpret the central limit theorem for sums as stating that, for large n, ΣX is approximately normal with mean nμX and standard deviation σX√n.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sums): For large n, ΣX is approximately ____.
**Answer**: a normal distribution

#### LO 3: identify when increasing sample size justifies modeling ΣX with a normal distribution.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sums): When does increasing sample size justify modeling ΣX with a normal distribution?
A. When σX is small.
B. When n is large, even if the original distribution is not normal.
C. Only when the original distribution is normal, regardless of n.
D. When n = 1.
**Answer**: B

#### LO 4: calculate the total Σx for n observed values.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Random Variable ΣX): Given observations 3, 7, 2, and 8, what is Σx?
A. 18
B. 20
C. 24
D. 10
**Answer**: B

#### LO 5: interpret ΣX as the sum of n values drawn from the original distribution, distinct from a single observation.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Random Variable ΣX): ΣX represents the _____ of n values drawn from the original distribution of X.
**Answer**: sum (total)

#### LO 6: state that the mean of the sums distribution equals n times the original mean.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Mean Of Sums): The mean of the sums distribution equals ____.
**Answer**: n times the mean of the original distribution (nμX)

#### LO 7: calculate the mean of the sums distribution as nμX for given μX and n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Mean Of Sums): If μX = 2.5 and n = 12, what is the mean of the sums distribution?
A. 2.5
B. 30
C. 12
D. 14.5
**Answer**: B

#### LO 8: define the standard deviation of the sums distribution as the original standard deviation multiplied by the square root of n.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Standard Deviation Of Sums): The standard deviation of the sums distribution is the original standard deviation multiplied by the _____ of n.
**Answer**: square root of n

#### LO 9: calculate the standard deviation of the sums distribution as σX√n for given σX and n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Standard Deviation Of Sums): If σX = 5 and n = 9, what is the standard deviation of the sums distribution?
A. 45
B. 15
C. 5
D. 3
**Answer**: B

#### LO 10: calculate the z-score for an observed sum using z = (Σx − nμX)/(σX√n).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Z-Score For Sums): Given μX = 5, σX = 2, n = 9, and an observed sum Σx = 54, what is z?
A. 0.5
B. 1.5
C. -1.5
D. 6
**Answer**: B

#### LO 11: use normalcdf(lower, upper, n×mean, √n×standard deviation) to find probabilities for sums.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Normalcdf For Sums): For μX = 10, σX = 4, n = 25, which calculator call finds P(200 ≤ ΣX ≤ 270)?
A. normalcdf(200, 270, 10, 20)
B. normalcdf(200, 270, 10, 4)
C. normalcdf(200, 270, 250, 20)
D. normalcdf(200, 270, 250, 4)
**Answer**: C

#### LO 12: use invNorm(area to the left, n×mean, √n×standard deviation) to find percentiles for sums.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: InvNorm For Sums): To find the 90th percentile of ΣX for μX = 6, σX = 3, n = 16, which call is correct?
A. invNorm(0.10, 96, 12)
B. invNorm(0.90, 6, 3)
C. invNorm(0.90, 96, 3)
D. invNorm(0.90, 96, 12)
**Answer**: D

#### LO 13: interpret an invNorm output as the kth percentile of the distribution of ΣX in context.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: InvNorm For Sums): What does invNorm(0.25, n×mean, √n×standard deviation) return for ΣX?
A. the mean of ΣX
B. the probability between two sums

D. a z-score
**Answer**: C

## Section: seg_93

#### LO 1: use the central limit theorem for sample means to compute probabilities and percentiles for a sample mean given the population mean, population standard deviation, and sample size
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sample Means): For a population with mean μ = 50 and standard deviation σ = 12, and sample size n = 36, which expression correctly standardizes P(X̄ > 53) using the CLT for sample means?
A. P(Z > (53 − 50)/12)
B. P(Z > (53 − 50)/(12/√36))
C. P(Z > (53 − 50)/(√36·12))
D. P(Z > (53 − 50)/√12)
**Answer**: B

#### LO 2: interpret probabilities and percentiles for a sample mean in context by describing the proportion of sample means below or above a specified value
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sample Means): If P(X̄ ≤ a) = 0.84 for samples of size n from a population, what does 0.84 represent?
A. About 84% of individual observations are ≤ a.
B. About 84% of sample means (from samples of size n) are ≤ a.
C. The population mean μ is ≤ a with probability 0.84.
D. About 84% of sample sums are ≤ a.
**Answer**: B

#### LO 3: identify the parameters of the normal approximation for totals as mean n×mu and standard deviation sqrt(n)×sigma for large samples
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sums): For large n, if each X has mean μ = 8 and standard deviation σ = 4, and you consider the total ΣX over n = 25, what are the mean and standard deviation of ΣX under the CLT for sums?
A. Mean 8, SD 4
B. Mean 200, SD 100
C. Mean 200, SD 20
D. Mean 8, SD 20
**Answer**: C

#### LO 4: calculate probabilities and percentiles for totals using the normal approximation provided by the central limit theorem for sums
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sums): With μ = 8, σ = 4, and n = 25, which expression correctly approximates P(ΣX ≤ 210) using the CLT for sums?
A. P(Z ≤ (210 − 25·8)/4)
B. P(Z ≤ (210 − 25·8)/(√25·4))
C. P(Z ≤ (210 − 8)/(√25·4))
D. P(Z ≤ (210 − 25·8)/(25·4))
**Answer**: B

#### LO 5: interpret probabilities and percentiles for totals by explaining what proportion of sample sums fall at or below a specified cutoff
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sums): If your CLT-for-sums calculation gives P(ΣX ≤ k) = 0.30 for samples of size n, what does 0.30 mean?
A. About 30% of individual observations are ≤ k.
B. About 30% of sample means (size n) are ≤ k.
C. About 30% of sample sums (over n observations) are ≤ k.
D. The population mean μ is ≤ k with probability 0.30.
**Answer**: C

#### LO 6: identify whether a question requires the central limit theorem for means or sums, or the exact distribution for an individual value
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Appropriate Use Of The CLT): Which approach is appropriate for finding P(X > c) for a single observation X?
A. Use the CLT for sample means.
B. Use the CLT for sums.
C. Use the exact distribution of X (do not use the CLT).
D. Use the CLT and then divide by n.
**Answer**: C

#### LO 7: differentiate between problems about individual values and those about sample means or sums, selecting the appropriate distribution in each case
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Appropriate Use Of The CLT): Which prompt clearly refers to a sample mean and thus suggests using the CLT for means?
A. Find P(X ≥ 10).
B. Find P(ΣX ≥ 200).
C. Find P(X̄ ≥ 10).
D. Find the probability a single X is below 10.
**Answer**: C

#### LO 8: explain why probabilities for individual values and for sample means differ due to using different distributions and standard errors
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Appropriate Use Of The CLT): Complete: Use the CLT when finding probabilities for means or sums; for individual values, do not use the CLT and instead use the ____.
**Answer**: variable’s own distribution

#### LO 9: define the law of large numbers as the tendency of the sample mean to approach the population mean as sample size increases
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Law Of Large Numbers): As sample size increases, the sample mean x̄ ______.
**Answer**: approaches the population mean μ

#### LO 10: explain how the central limit theorem illustrates the law of large numbers through increasingly normal sample means and decreasing standard error as n grows
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Law Of Large Numbers): Which statement best connects the CLT with the law of large numbers?
A. As n increases, individual X values become normal with fixed standard deviation σ.
B. As n increases, the sampling distribution of x̄ becomes more normal and its standard deviation decreases as σ/√n.
C. As n increases, μ increases and σ decreases to zero.
D. As n increases, the sum ΣX has a fixed standard deviation σ.
**Answer**: B

#### LO 11: calculate the standard error of the mean as sigma divided by the square root of n for a given population standard deviation and sample size
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Standard Error Of The Mean): If σ = 15 and n = 25, what is the standard error of X̄?
A. 15/√25 = 3
B. √(15/25)
C. 15/25 = 0.6
D. √25/15 = 5/15
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: explain how increasing the sample size reduces the standard error and decreases the variability of sample means
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Standard Error Of The Mean): The standard deviation of the sampling distribution of X̄ equals ____ and decreases as n increases.
**Answer**: σ/√n

#### LO 13: use the normal approximation to the binomial to find probabilities when np and nq are sufficiently large, with mean np and standard deviation sqrt(npq)
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Normal Approximation To The Binomial): For X ~ B(n = 200, p = 0.40), which normal distribution approximates X when np and nq are sufficiently large?
A. Mean μ = 200, SD σ = √(200)
B. Mean μ = 0.40, SD σ = √(0.40·0.60)
C. Mean μ = 200·0.40, SD σ = √(200·0.40·0.60)
D. Mean μ = 200/0.40, SD σ = √(200/0.40)
**Answer**: C

#### LO 14: compare exact binomial probabilities with normal-approximation results and comment on their agreement
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Normal Approximation To The Binomial): In which scenario would you expect the normal approximation to the binomial to agree most closely with the exact probabilities?
A. n = 20, p = 0.05
B. n = 50, p = 0.50
C. n = 20, p = 0.90
D. n = 30, p = 0.02
**Answer**: B

#### LO 15: define the continuity correction factor as adjusting a discrete x by plus or minus 0.5 when using a continuous normal approximation to the binomial
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Continuity Correction Factor): When using a continuous normal approximation to a binomial X, adjust x by ____ to improve the approximation.
**Answer**: ±0.5

#### LO 16: use the continuity correction factor to translate binomial statements into normal bounds, such as X >= k becoming Y >= k - 0.5
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Continuity Correction Factor): Let X be binomial and Y its normal approximation. Which normal bound corresponds to P(X ≥ 20)?
A. P(Y ≥ 19.5)
B. P(Y ≥ 20.5)
C. P(Y > 20.5)
D. P(Y > 19.5)
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_97

#### LO 1: calculate parameters and probabilities for X̄ and ΣX using X̄ ~ N(mu, sigma/sqrt(n)) and ΣX ~ N(n·mu, sqrt(n)·sigma) when n is sufficiently large.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Central Limit Theorem): For a population with μ = 50 and σ = 10, and sample size n = 25, which distributions are correct for X̄ and ΣX under the CLT?
A. X̄ ~ N(50, 2), ΣX ~ N(1250, 50)
B. X̄ ~ N(50, 10), ΣX ~ N(250, 10)
C. X̄ ~ N(1250, 50), ΣX ~ N(50, 2)
D. X̄ ~ N(0, 1), ΣX ~ N(0, 1)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: state the conditions and results of the central limit theorem for sample means and sums, including their approximate normal forms, means, and standard deviations.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Central Limit Theorem): According to the Central Limit Theorem, for sufficiently large n, the sample mean X̄ is approximately ____.
**Answer**: N(μ, σ/√n)

#### LO 3: explain why the sampling distributions of means and sums become approximately normal as n increases and how their centers and spreads relate to mu and sigma.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Central Limit Theorem): As n increases, which statement correctly describes the sampling distributions of X̄ and ΣX?
A. Both become approximately normal; X̄ centers at μ with spread σ/√n; ΣX centers at nμ with spread √n·σ.
B. Both become uniform with spreads σ and nσ.
C. X̄ becomes normal centered at nμ and ΣX centered at μ.
D. Shapes do not change with n; spreads stay equal to σ.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 4: state that for large n, X̄ is approximately N(mu, sigma/sqrt(n)) with mean equal to the population mean.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sample Means (Averages)): For large n, the distribution of sample means is approximately ____ with mean equal to the population mean.
**Answer**: N(μ, σ/√n)

#### LO 5: calculate the standard error sigma/sqrt(n), z-scores, percentiles, and probabilities for sample means.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sample Means (Averages)): For a population with σ = 20 and sample size n = 16, what is the standard error σ/√n of X̄?
A. 5
B. 20
C. 4
D. 80
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: compare the histogram of the population to histograms of sample means (for n = 5 versus n = 10) to assess changes in shape and variability predicted by the CLT.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sample Means (Averages)): Comparing histograms of sample means for n = 5 versus n = 10, which change is predicted by the CLT?
A. The n = 10 histogram is more bell-shaped and has smaller variability than the n = 5 histogram.
B. The n = 10 histogram is more skewed and has larger variability.
C. Both histograms match the population exactly regardless of n.
D. The n = 5 histogram is more bell-shaped with smaller variability than the n = 10 histogram.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 7: calculate the mean n·mu, standard deviation sqrt(n)·sigma, z-scores, and probabilities for sample sums using the CLT.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sums): For a population with μ = 8, σ = 3, and n = 36, what are the mean and standard deviation of ΣX under the CLT?
A. Mean 288; SD 18
B. Mean 48; SD 3
C. Mean 8; SD 0.5
D. Mean 36; SD 108
**Answer**: A

#### LO 8: state that for large n, ΣX is approximately N(n·mu, sqrt(n)·sigma) regardless of the population’s shape.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Central Limit Theorem For Sums): For large n, ΣX is approximately ____ regardless of the population’s shape.
**Answer**: N(nμ, √n·σ)

#### LO 9: define a sampling distribution as the probability distribution of a statistic computed from all simple random samples of size n from a population.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sampling Distribution): A sampling distribution is the ____.
**Answer**: probability distribution of a statistic

#### LO 10: construct an empirical sampling distribution by aggregating sample means from repeated random samples and sketching a histogram.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sampling Distribution): Which action constructs an empirical sampling distribution of sample means?
A. Take many random samples of size n, compute each sample mean, and plot a histogram of those means.
B. Take one random sample of size n and plot a histogram of the raw data.
C. Plot a histogram of the entire population values.
D. Compute the population mean once and report it.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 11: define the standard error of the mean as the standard deviation of the sampling distribution of X̄, equal to sigma/sqrt(n).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Standard Error Of The Mean): The standard error of the mean is the ____ of the sampling distribution of X̄, equal to σ/√n.
**Answer**: standard deviation

#### LO 12: calculate the standard error sigma/sqrt(n) for a given population standard deviation and sample size.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Standard Error Of The Mean): If σ = 12 and n = 9, what is the standard error of X̄?
A. 4
B. 3
C. 12
D. 36
**Answer**: A

#### LO 13: define the normal distribution with parameters mu and sigma and notation X ~ N(mu, sigma), including the standard normal case mu = 0 and sigma = 1.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Normal Distribution): The standard normal is the case ____.
**Answer**: μ = 0, σ = 1

#### LO 14: calculate normal probabilities and percentiles by standardizing with a z-score when mu and sigma are known.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Normal Distribution): If X ~ N(100, 15), what is the z-score for x = 130?
A. 2
B. -2
C. 30
D. 15
**Answer**: A

#### LO 15: define the population mean mu and sample mean x̄ as the sum of values divided by the number of values.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Mean): The sample mean x̄ equals the ____.
**Answer**: sum of sample values divided by the number of sample values

#### LO 16: calculate x̄ and mu from raw data such as the cookie recipe durations.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Mean): Given population values 2, 4, 6, 8 and a simple random sample 4, 8, what are μ and x̄?
A. μ = 5, x̄ = 6
B. μ = 6, x̄ = 5
C. μ = 5, x̄ = 5
D. μ = 6, x̄ = 6
**Answer**: A

#### LO 17: calculate the z-score for a sample mean using z = (x̄ − mu)/(sigma/sqrt(n)).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Z-Score For Sample Means): For μ = 50, σ = 12, n = 9, and observed x̄ = 54, what is z = (x̄ − μ)/(σ/√n)?
A. 1
B. 0.75
C. 3
D. -1
**Answer**: A

#### LO 18: state the z-score formula for sample means: z = (x̄ − mu)/(sigma/sqrt(n)).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Z-Score For Sample Means): The z-score formula for a sample mean is z = ____.
**Answer**: (x̄ − μ)/(σ/√n)

#### LO 19: calculate the z-score for a sample sum using z = (Σx − n·mu)/(sqrt(n)·sigma).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Z-Score For Sums): For μ = 5, σ = 2, n = 25, and Σx = 140, what is z = (Σx − n·μ)/(√n·σ)?
A. 1.5
B. -1.5
C. 3
D. 0.15
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_101

#### LO 1: Calculate a z-interval for a population mean when σ is known using x̄ ± EBM.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Confidence Interval For A Population Mean): Given x̄ = 50, σ = 10, n = 25, and z_{α/2} = 1.96, which is the z-interval for μ?
A. (46.08, 53.92)
B. (48.04, 51.96)
C. (46.84, 53.16)
D. (40.00, 60.00)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: Define the point estimate of μ as the sample mean x̄.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Point Estimate Of The Population Mean): The point estimate of μ is the ____.
**Answer**: sample mean x̄

#### LO 3: Calculate the sample mean x̄ from sample data to serve as the point estimate of μ.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Point Estimate Of The Population Mean): For sample data 5, 7, 9, what is x̄?
A. 6
B. 7
C. 7.5
D. 8
**Answer**: B

#### LO 4: Define the error bound for a mean as EBM = z_{α/2} · (σ/√n).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Error Bound For A Population Mean (EBM)): For a mean with known σ, EBM = ____.
**Answer**: z_{α/2} · (σ/√n)

#### LO 5: Calculate EBM given σ, n, and a stated confidence level via z_{α/2}.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Error Bound For A Population Mean (EBM)): If σ = 12, n = 36, and z_{α/2} = 2.00, what is EBM?
A. 2
B. 4
C. 6
D. 24
**Answer**: B

#### LO 6: Define the confidence level as CL = 1 − α and as the percent of intervals that capture the true parameter under repeated sampling.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Confidence Level (CL)): The confidence level satisfies ____.
**Answer**: CL = 1 − α

#### LO 7: Calculate CL or α using CL = 1 − α.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Confidence Level (CL)): If CL = 0.92, what is α?
A. 0.04
B. 0.08
C. 0.12
D. 0.92
**Answer**: B

#### LO 8: Define alpha as the probability that the interval does not contain the true parameter, with α + CL = 1.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Alpha (α)): Alpha (α) is the ____ that the confidence interval does not contain the true parameter.
**Answer**: probability

#### LO 9: Calculate α/2 for a two-sided interval given α.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Alpha (α)): For α = 0.10 in a two-sided interval, α/2 equals:
A. 0.10
B. 0.05
C. 0.90
D. 0.95
**Answer**: B

#### LO 10: Describe how α is split equally into α/2 in each tail of the standard normal distribution for a two-sided confidence interval.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Alpha (α)): In a two-sided z-interval, how is α allocated in the standard normal tails?
A. All α is in the right tail.
B. All α is in the left tail.
C. α/2 in each tail.
D. 1 − α/2 in each tail.
**Answer**: C

#### LO 11: Define z_{α/2} as the standard normal critical value with right-tail area α/2 (left-tail area 1 − α/2).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Z-Score For Confidence Level (z_{α/2})): z_{α/2} is the critical value with right-tail area ____ (left-tail area 1 − α/2).
**Answer**: α/2

#### LO 12: Calculate z_{α/2} for a given confidence level using a Z table or invNorm with left-tail area 1 − α/2.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Z-Score For Confidence Level (z_{α/2})): To find z_{α/2} with technology, which area should be used as input to invNorm?
A. α/2
B. 1 − α/2
C. 1 − α
D. α
**Answer**: B

#### LO 13: Calculate the standard error σ/√n from given σ and n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Standard Error Of The Mean): If σ = 15 and n = 25, what is the standard error of x̄?
A. 2.5
B. 3
C. 5
D. 15
**Answer**: B

#### LO 14: State that x̄ is approximately normal with mean μ and standard deviation σ/√n when σ is known.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sampling Distribution Of The Sample Mean): When σ is known, x̄ is (approximately) ____ with mean μ and standard deviation σ/√n.
**Answer**: normally distributed

#### LO 15: Use the normal distribution of x̄ with known σ to justify constructing a z-interval for μ.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sampling Distribution Of The Sample Mean): Which statement justifies using a z-interval for μ when σ is known?
A. x̄ has a normal sampling distribution with standard deviation σ/√n.
B. x̄ always equals μ for any sample.
C. The population distribution must be exactly uniform.
D. σ is estimated from the sample standard deviation s.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 16: Identify when a z-interval for a mean is appropriate: data from a random sample and known population σ.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Conditions For Using A Z-Interval For A Mean): When is a z-interval for μ appropriate?
A. When data come from a random sample and σ is known.
B. When data are convenience sampled and σ is unknown.
C. When n is small and σ is unknown.
D. When the median is the parameter of interest.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 17: Interpret a confidence interval using the template 'We estimate with ___% confidence that the true mean (context) is between ___ and ___.'
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Interpretation Of A Confidence Interval): Template: "We estimate with _____ that the true population mean (context) is between ___ and ___."
**Answer**: ___% confidence

#### LO 18: Calculate and compare EBM or interval widths for different confidence levels while holding σ, n, and x̄ fixed.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Effect Of Changing The Confidence Level): Holding σ, n, and x̄ fixed, which confidence level yields the wider interval?
A. 90%
B. 95%
C. 99%
D. All give the same width
**Answer**: C

#### LO 19: Describe how increasing CL increases EBM and widens the interval, while decreasing CL narrows it.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Effect Of Changing The Confidence Level): Increasing the confidence level ____ the interval.
**Answer**: widens

#### LO 20: Describe how increasing n decreases EBM and narrows the interval, while decreasing n increases EBM and widens the interval.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Effect Of Changing The Sample Size): Increasing the sample size n ____ EBM.
**Answer**: decreases

#### LO 21: Calculate and compare EBM for different sample sizes with σ and CL fixed.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Effect Of Changing The Sample Size): With σ and CL fixed, which sample size gives the smaller EBM?
A. n = 25
B. n = 50
C. n = 100
D. They all give the same EBM
**Answer**: C

#### LO 22: Calculate the error bound and sample mean from interval endpoints using EBM = (upper − lower)/2 and x̄ = (upper + lower)/2.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Working Backwards From A Confidence Interval): For a confidence interval (18, 22), what are EBM and x̄?
A. EBM = 2, x̄ = 20
B. EBM = 4, x̄ = 20
C. EBM = 2, x̄ = 22
D. EBM = 20, x̄ = 2
**Answer**: A

#### LO 23: Calculate the required sample size n = (z^2 σ^2)/(EBM^2) for a desired margin of error at a given confidence level, rounding up.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sample Size For A Desired Margin Of Error): For z = 2, σ = 10, and desired EBM = 3, what required n (round up)?
A. 44
B. 45
C. 50
D. 400/9
**Answer**: B

#### LO 24: State the rule to round the computed sample size up to the next whole number.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sample Size For A Desired Margin Of Error): When computing required sample size for a desired margin of error, always round ____.
**Answer**: up to the next integer


---
## Section: seg_103

#### LO 1: Describe the shape and key properties of the Student's t-distribution, including symmetry about zero, heavier tails than the standard normal, and dependence on degrees of freedom.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Student's T-Distribution): Compared to the standard normal, the Student's t-distribution has ______.
**Answer**: heavier tails

#### LO 2: Calculate t critical values or tail probabilities for specified degrees of freedom using a calculator function (tcdf/invT) or a t-table.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Student's T-Distribution): To obtain a t critical value for a given df when σ is unknown and s is used, which distribution should you use?
A. Student's t-distribution
B. Standard normal distribution
C. Chi-square distribution
D. Uniform distribution
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: Identify scenarios where the population standard deviation is unknown and s estimates σ, indicating the Student's t-distribution should be used regardless of sample size.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: When To Use Student's T-Distribution): Use the Student's t-distribution whenever the population standard deviation is unknown and s estimates σ, ______.
**Answer**: regardless of sample size

#### LO 4: Explain why the Student's t-distribution is used instead of the normal distribution when σ is unknown, including issues with small samples and dependence on sample size.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: When To Use Student's T-Distribution): Why use the Student's t-distribution instead of the normal distribution when σ is unknown?
A. Because σ is unknown and s is used to estimate it, so t applies regardless of sample size
B. Because t applies only when n < 30 and σ is known
C. Because t applies only when σ is known and s is not used
D. Because t is used only for proportions when σ is unknown
**Answer**: A

#### LO 5: Define the t-score as t = (x̄ − μ) / (s / √n) for samples from an approximately normal population with unknown σ.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: T-Score): The t-score is the standardized statistic t = ______.
**Answer**: (x̄ − μ) / (s / √n)

#### LO 6: Calculate a t-score from sample data given x̄, μ, s, and n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: T-Score): Given x̄ = 22, μ = 20, s = 6, and n = 9, what is the t-score?
A. 1
B. 0.33
C. 3
D. -1
**Answer**: A

#### LO 7: Interpret a t-score as how many s/√n units the sample mean lies from the population mean.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: T-Score): A t-score measures how many ______ the sample mean is from the population mean.
**Answer**: s/√n units

#### LO 8: Define degrees of freedom as df = n − 1 arising from the calculation of the sample standard deviation.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom): Degrees of freedom are defined as df = ______.
**Answer**: n − 1

#### LO 9: Calculate the degrees of freedom for a given sample size n as n − 1.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom): For a sample size n = 15, what are the degrees of freedom?
A. 13
B. 14
C. 15
D. 16
**Answer**: B

#### LO 10: Describe how the t-distribution has mean 0, is symmetric, has heavier tails than the standard normal, and approaches the normal as degrees of freedom increase.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Properties Of The Student's T-Distribution): As degrees of freedom increase, the Student's t-distribution ______.
**Answer**: approaches the standard normal

#### LO 11: State that the Student's t-distribution is centered at zero, symmetric, and more spread out than the standard normal.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Properties Of The Student's T-Distribution): Which statement correctly describes the Student's t-distribution?
A. Centered at 0, symmetric, and more spread out than the standard normal
B. Centered at 0, skewed, and narrower than the standard normal
C. Centered at μ (unknown), symmetric, and identical to the standard normal
D. Identical to the standard normal for any degrees of freedom
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: Identify the assumptions needed for using the Student's t-distribution: a simple random sample from an approximately normal population with unknown μ and σ.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Assumptions For Using Student's T-Distribution): Assumptions include a simple random sample from a population that is ______ with unknown μ and σ.
**Answer**: approximately normally distributed

#### LO 13: Define the error bound for the mean when σ is unknown as EBM = t_(α/2) × s / √n with df = n − 1.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Error Bound For The Mean (EBM)): When σ is unknown, the error bound for the mean is EBM = ______ (with df = n − 1).
**Answer**: t_(α/2) × s / √n

#### LO 14: Calculate the error bound for the mean given a confidence level, sample standard deviation, sample size, and degrees of freedom.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Error Bound For The Mean (EBM)): Given t_(α/2) = 2.13, s = 8, n = 16, and df = 15, what is EBM?
A. 4.26
B. 2.13
C. 1.00
D. 8.52
**Answer**: A

#### LO 15: Calculate a confidence interval for a population mean using x̄ ± EBM when σ is unknown.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Confidence Interval For A Population Mean (σ Unknown)): If x̄ = 75 and EBM = 5, what is the confidence interval?
A. (70, 80)
B. (72.5, 77.5)
C. (75, 80)
D. (65, 85)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 16: Use a Student's t table to find t critical values for specified degrees of freedom and confidence levels or tail areas.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Student's T Table): A Student's t table provides t critical values for specified degrees of freedom and ______.
**Answer**: confidence levels or tail areas

#### LO 17: Interpret the headings and entries of a t table, including how columns represent confidence levels or tail areas and rows represent degrees of freedom.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Student's T Table): How are entries organized in a Student's t table?
A. Rows are degrees of freedom; columns are confidence levels or tail areas
B. Rows are confidence levels; columns are degrees of freedom
C. Rows are sample means; columns are standard deviations
D. Rows are probabilities; columns are z-scores
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_105

#### LO 1: Calculate a confidence interval estimate for the population proportion p from sample data using p′ ± EBP.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Population Proportion): Given p′ = 0.60 and EBP = 0.10, what is the confidence interval for p?
A. (0.50, 0.70)
B. (0.60, 0.80)
C. (0.40, 0.60)
D. (0.55, 0.65)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: Interpret a confidence interval for the population proportion p in the context of a real-world problem.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Population Proportion): A confidence interval for p is used to estimate the _____.
**Answer**: true proportion of successes in a population

#### LO 3: Identify p as the true proportion of successes that is the parameter of interest in a binomial setting.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Population Proportion): In a binomial setting, p denotes the _____.
**Answer**: true proportion of successes

#### LO 4: Define the binomial random variable X ~ B(n, p) as the count of successes in n trials with success probability p.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Binomial Distribution For Counts): The binomial random variable X ~ B(n, p) models the _____ in n independent trials with success probability p.
**Answer**: number of successes

#### LO 5: Describe the binomial model for counts of successes and its notation X ~ B(n, p).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Binomial Distribution For Counts): Which notation correctly expresses the binomial model for the count of successes?
A. X ~ B(n, p)
B. P′ ~ Normal(p, √pq/n)
C. p = X/n
D. The mean µ ~ N(0, 1)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: Define the sample proportion p′ (p-hat) as X/n, a point estimate of p.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sample Proportion (p′ Or p̂)): The sample proportion p′ (p-hat) is defined as _____.
**Answer**: X/n

#### LO 7: Calculate the sample proportion p′ from the number of successes x and sample size n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sample Proportion (p′ Or p̂)): In a sample with x = 18 successes out of n = 30, p′ equals:
A. 0.60
B. 0.30
C. 0.40
D. 0.18
**Answer**: A

#### LO 8: Define the complement proportions q = 1 − p and q′ = 1 − p′.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Complement Proportions (q And q′)): The complement proportion q is defined by q = _____.
**Answer**: 1 − p

#### LO 9: Compute q or q′ as 1 minus the corresponding proportion p or p′.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Complement Proportions (q And q′)): If p′ = 0.35, then q′ equals:
A. 0.65
B. 0.55
C. 0.35
D. 0.75
**Answer**: A

#### LO 10: Identify when the sampling distribution of P′ is approximately normal (large n with p not near 0 or 1).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Normal Approximation For Sample Proportion): When is the sampling distribution of P′ approximately normal?
A. When n is large and p is not near 0 or 1.
B. For any n whenever x ≥ 5.
C. Only when p = 0.5 regardless of n.
D. Only when n is small and p is near 0 or 1.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 11: Calculate the mean (p) and standard deviation sqrt(pq/n) of the approximate normal distribution of P′ when appropriate.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Normal Approximation For Sample Proportion): If p = 0.50 and n = 100, what are the mean and standard deviation of the approximate normal distribution of P′?
A. Mean 0.50; SD 0.05
B. Mean 0.50; SD 0.50
C. Mean 0.05; SD 0.50
D. Mean 0.50; SD √(0.50/100)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: State the z-score formula for a sample proportion as z = (p′ − p) / sqrt(pq/n).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Z-Score For Sample Proportion): The z-score for a sample proportion is z = _____.
**Answer**: (p′ − p) / √(pq/n)

#### LO 13: Calculate the z-score for a sample proportion given p′, p, and n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Z-Score For Sample Proportion): If p = 0.50, n = 100, and p′ = 0.56, what is z?
A. 1.20
B. 0.06
C. 0.50
D. 2.40
**Answer**: A

#### LO 14: Compute a confidence interval for p using p′ ± z_{alpha/2} sqrt(p′ q′ / n).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Confidence Interval For A Population Proportion): With p′ = 0.50, n = 100, and z_{α/2} = 2, what is the confidence interval for p?
A. (0.40, 0.60)
B. (0.45, 0.55)
C. (0.50, 0.70)
D. (0.35, 0.65)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 15: Interpret a confidence interval for p, including what the stated confidence level implies about repeated intervals.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Confidence Interval For A Population Proportion): The stated confidence level is the _____.
**Answer**: long-run proportion of such intervals that contain the true p

#### LO 16: Define a confidence interval for a population proportion as the estimate p′ plus or minus the error bound EBP.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Confidence Interval For A Population Proportion): A confidence interval for a population proportion is p′ plus or minus the _____.
**Answer**: error bound EBP

#### LO 17: Define the error bound for a proportion as EBP = z_{alpha/2} sqrt(p′ q′ / n), using p′ and q′ because p and q are unknown.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Error Bound For A Proportion (EBP)): The error bound for a proportion is EBP = _____.
**Answer**: z_{α/2} √(p′ q′ / n)

#### LO 18: Calculate the error bound EBP for a given confidence level using sample data p′, q′, and n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Error Bound For A Proportion (EBP)): Given p′ = 0.50, q′ = 0.50, n = 400, and z_{α/2} = 2, what is EBP?
A. 0.05
B. 0.10
C. 0.025
D. 0.20
**Answer**: A

#### LO 19: State that the normal-based confidence interval for a proportion is valid only if np′ > 5 and nq′ > 5.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Conditions For Proportion Confidence Interval): The normal-based confidence interval for a proportion is valid only if _____.
**Answer**: np′ > 5 and nq′ > 5

#### LO 20: Identify problems that involve a population proportion as those focusing on counts of successes with no mention of a mean or average.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Identifying A Proportion Problem): A problem involves a population proportion when it focuses on _____ with no mention of a mean or average.
**Answer**: counts of successes

#### LO 21: Classify a scenario as a binomial proportion problem when it concerns the number of successes out of n trials.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Identifying A Proportion Problem): Which scenario is a binomial proportion problem?
A. Out of n trials, how many successes occur?
B. What is the average time to complete a task?
C. What is the standard deviation of heights?
D. What is the median income?
**Answer**: A

#### LO 22: Differentiate proportion problems from mean problems by determining whether the parameter is a success probability p or a population mean.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Identifying A Proportion Problem): Which parameter indicates a proportion problem rather than a mean problem?
A. A success probability p
B. A population mean
C. A sample mean
D. An average over measurements
**Answer**: A

#### LO 23: Calculate a plus-four confidence interval by using x + 2 successes and n + 4 trials when confidence level is at least 90% and sample size is at least 10.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: “Plus-Four” Confidence Interval For p): Using the plus-four method with x = 10, n = 20, and z_{α/2} = 2, what is the confidence interval? (Use x + 2 and n + 4.)
A. (0.296, 0.704)
B. (0.400, 0.600)
C. (0.333, 0.667)
D. (0.250, 0.750)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 24: Identify when to apply the plus-four adjustment to improve the accuracy of a proportion confidence interval.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: “Plus-Four” Confidence Interval For p): When should the plus-four adjustment be applied?
A. When confidence level ≥ 90% and sample size ≥ 10.
B. Only when p is near 0 or 1 for any n.
C. When confidence level < 90% regardless of n.
D. Only when n < 10.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 25: Calculate the required sample size n = (z_{alpha/2}^2 p′ q′) / EBP^2 for a desired margin of error at a specified confidence level.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sample Size For Estimating A Proportion): For desired EBP = 0.10 with z_{α/2} = 2 and p′ = q′ = 0.50, what required sample size n results?
A. 100
B. 50
C. 200
D. 25
**Answer**: A

#### LO 26: Explain how rearranging the error bound formula yields the sample size equation for estimating a population proportion.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sample Size For Estimating A Proportion): Rearranging EBP = z_{α/2} √(p′ q′ / n) gives the sample size equation n = _____.
**Answer**: (z_{α/2}^2 p′ q′) / EBP^2

#### LO 27: Explain why setting p′ = q′ = 0.5 maximizes p′q′ = 0.25 and produces the most conservative (largest) required sample size when p′ is unknown.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Conservative Choice Of p′ For Sample Size): Choosing p′ = q′ = 0.5 is conservative because it maximizes p′q′ to _____.
**Answer**: 0.25

#### LO 28: Use p′ = q′ = 0.5 to compute a conservative required sample size when there is no prior estimate of p.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Conservative Choice Of p′ For Sample Size): With no prior estimate of p, using p′ = q′ = 0.5, z_{α/2} = 2, and desired EBP = 0.05, what n results?
A. 400
B. 200
C. 100
D. 800
**Answer**: A

#### LO 29: Calculate alpha from a given confidence level and determine the corresponding z_{alpha/2} critical value.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Confidence Level And α): For confidence level CL = 0.92, which pair is correct?
A. α = 0.08; z_{α/2} = z_{0.04}
B. α = 0.04; z_{α/2} = z_{0.08}
C. α = 0.92; z_{α/2} = z_{0.46}
D. α = 0.08; z_{α/2} = z_{0.08}
**Answer**: A

#### LO 30: State the relationship alpha = 1 − CL used in constructing confidence intervals for proportions.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Confidence Level And α): The relationship between confidence level and alpha is _____.
**Answer**: α = 1 − CL

#### LO 31: Explain the long-run interpretation of a confidence level as the proportion of such intervals that would contain the true p in repeated sampling.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Confidence Level And α): A confidence level is interpreted as the _____ that would contain the true p in repeated sampling.
**Answer**: long-run proportion of such intervals that contain the true p

## Section: seg_107

#### LO 1: calculate a confidence interval for the mean cost of a home using sample data and an appropriate distribution.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Confidence Interval): When you calculate a confidence interval for the mean cost of a home, what form should you report?
A. A single point estimate equal to the sample mean
B. An interval estimate for the mean cost of a home at a stated confidence level
C. The population standard deviation of home costs
D. A probability that the sample mean equals the population mean
**Answer**: B

#### LO 2: interpret the confidence interval for the mean home cost in context.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Confidence Interval): A Confidence Interval is an ______ for the mean cost of a home at a stated confidence level.
**Answer**: interval estimate

#### LO 3: calculate the confidence level from a given alpha or tail area.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Confidence Level): Given alpha (α) as the combined area in both tails, the confidence level equals:
A. α/2
B. 1 - α
C. 1 - α/2
D. α
**Answer**: B

#### LO 4: explain what a stated confidence level means in terms of long-run capture of the population mean.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Confidence Level): The Confidence Level is the specified ______ associated with the interval (e.g., 90%, 95%, 99%).
**Answer**: percentage

#### LO 5: calculate alpha as the combined tail area outside a specified confidence interval.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Alpha (α)): Alpha (α) is:
A. The area within the confidence interval
B. The combined area in both tails outside the confidence interval
C. The area in each tail outside the confidence interval
D. The sample mean of the data
**Answer**: B

#### LO 6: state the value of alpha for a given confidence level.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Alpha (α)): Alpha (α) is the ______ in both tails outside the confidence interval.
**Answer**: combined area

#### LO 7: calculate the area in each tail (alpha divided by two) for a given confidence level.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Tail Area (α/2)): Tail Area (α/2) refers to:
A. The total area outside the interval across both tails
B. The area in each tail outside the confidence interval
C. The area within the interval
D. The probability that the population mean equals the sample mean
**Answer**: B

#### LO 8: identify the tail regions corresponding to alpha over two on a confidence interval diagram.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Tail Area (α/2)): Tail Area (α/2) is the area in ______ outside the confidence interval.
**Answer**: each tail

#### LO 9: interpret alpha over two as the probability of falling beyond each bound of the interval under the assumed model.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Tail Area (α/2)): Under the assumed model, α/2 represents:
A. The entire area outside the confidence interval
B. The area within the confidence interval
C. The area in each tail beyond the interval’s bound
D. The sample size divided by two
**Answer**: C

#### LO 10: calculate the error bound for the mean and use it to form the interval’s endpoints.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Error Bound For The Mean (EBM)): Error Bound For The Mean (EBM) is best described as:
A. The sample median of the home prices
B. The difference between the sample mean and population mean
C. The amount added to and subtracted from the sample mean to obtain the interval’s upper and lower limits
D. The area in each tail outside the confidence interval
**Answer**: C

#### LO 11: explain how the error bound is determined and what it represents in constructing the interval.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Error Bound For The Mean (EBM)): The Error Bound For The Mean (EBM) is the amount ______ to and ______ from the sample mean to obtain the interval’s upper and lower limits.
**Answer**: added to and subtracted

#### LO 12: compute the sample mean of the recorded home sale prices.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sample Mean (X̄)): The Sample Mean (X̄) is the ______ of the sample sale prices used in constructing the interval.
**Answer**: mean

#### LO 13: define the sample mean as the arithmetic average of the sampled home sale prices.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sample Mean (X̄)): Which statement defines Sample Mean (X̄)?
A. The mean of the sample sale prices used in constructing the interval
B. The median of the population sale prices
C. The lower endpoint of the confidence interval
D. The area outside the confidence interval
**Answer**: A

#### LO 14: compute the sample standard deviation of the recorded home sale prices.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sample Standard Deviation (Sx)): Sample Standard Deviation (Sx) is the ______ computed from the sample sale prices.
**Answer**: standard deviation

#### LO 15: compute the sample size by counting the number of recorded home sale prices.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sample Size (N)): Sample Size (N) is the ______ of sampled homes used in the analysis.
**Answer**: number

#### LO 16: define X̄ in words as the random variable representing the mean sale price from a random sample of homes.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Random Variable X̄): Random Variable X̄ is the ______ representing the mean sale price from a sample.
**Answer**: random variable

#### LO 17: state the estimated sampling distribution assumed for X̄ using words and symbols.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Estimated Distribution For X̄): Estimated Distribution For X̄ is the assumed ______ (in words and symbols) used to model X̄ when building the confidence interval.
**Answer**: distribution

#### LO 18: describe the assumed distribution for X̄ in words and symbols when constructing the confidence interval.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Estimated Distribution For X̄): Which best describes the Estimated Distribution For X̄?
A. The actual list of sample sale prices
B. The assumed distribution (in words and symbols) used to model X̄ when building the confidence interval
C. The lower and upper endpoints of the interval
D. The combined area in both tails outside the interval
**Answer**: B

#### LO 19: calculate the lower and upper limits of the confidence interval and mark them on a number line with the sample mean.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Confidence Interval Limits): Confidence Interval Limits are:
A. The sample mean and standard deviation
B. The lower and upper endpoints of the confidence interval placed on the number line
C. The confidence level and alpha
D. The areas in each tail outside the interval
**Answer**: B

#### LO 20: interpret the lower and upper limits as plausible bounds for the population mean home cost.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Confidence Interval Limits): Confidence Interval Limits are the lower and upper ______ of the confidence interval placed on the number line.
**Answer**: endpoints

#### LO 21: calculate the width of a confidence interval as the difference between its upper and lower limits.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Interval Width): Interval Width is defined as the:
A. Combined area in both tails outside the interval
B. Distance between the lower and upper limits of the confidence interval
C. Distance from the sample mean to the lower limit only
D. Confidence level expressed as a percentage
**Answer**: B

#### LO 22: compare interval widths across different confidence levels to determine how width changes as confidence increases.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Interval Width): When comparing intervals at different confidence levels, what quantity are you comparing to judge which is wider?
A. Alpha (α)
B. Tail Area (α/2)
C. The distance between the lower and upper limits of each interval
D. The sample size (N)
**Answer**: C

#### LO 23: interpret a confidence interval in plain language for a general audience and for this home-price study.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Interpretation Of A Confidence Interval): Interpretation Of A Confidence Interval is the ______ of what the interval conveys about the mean in general and for this study.
**Answer**: explanation

#### LO 24: explain how increasing the confidence level affects the error bound and the width of the interval, and why.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Effect Of Confidence Level On EBM And Width): Which option best matches the concept 'Effect Of Confidence Level On EBM And Width'?
A. How to compute sample mean and standard deviation
B. How changes in confidence level affect the error bound and the width of the interval
C. How to choose the number line scale for plotting intervals
D. How tail areas are split between both tails
**Answer**: B

## Section: seg_109

#### LO 1: Calculate the 90% confidence interval for the proportion of students in this school who were born in this state from sample data.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Confidence Interval): From sample data you found P′ = 0.62 and EBP = 0.04 at the 90% confidence level. What is the 90% confidence interval?
A. (0.58, 0.66)
B. (0.62, 0.66)
C. (0.56, 0.62)
D. (0.60, 0.64)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: Interpret a calculated confidence interval for the proportion of students born in this state in the context of the class survey.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Confidence Interval): A confidence interval is the ______ to be computed for the proportion of students in this school who were born in this state at a specified confidence level.
**Answer**: interval

#### LO 3: Calculate the error bound for the sample proportion associated with a specified confidence level.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Error Bound For A Proportion (EBP)): A sample gives a confidence interval [0.38, 0.46] for the proportion. What is the error bound (EBP)?
A. 0.04
B. 0.08
C. 0.42
D. 0.46
**Answer**: A

#### LO 4: Explain what the error bound represents in relation to the confidence interval for the sample proportion.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Error Bound For A Proportion (EBP)): The error bound is associated with the ______.
**Answer**: confidence interval for the sample proportion

#### LO 5: Calculate the confidence level when given the combined tail area α for a proportion confidence interval.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Confidence Level): If the combined tail area α is 0.10 for a proportion confidence interval, what is the confidence level?
A. 90%
B. 10%
C. 95%
D. 5%
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: Explain what a confidence level means and how it relates to the proportion confidence interval and its error bound.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Confidence Level): A confidence level is the specified ______ that determines the confidence interval and its error bound.
**Answer**: percentage

#### LO 7: Calculate the combined tail area α corresponding to a stated confidence level.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Combined Tail Area (Alpha)): For a 95% confidence level, what is the combined tail area α?
A. 0.05
B. 0.95
C. 0.025
D. 0.90
**Answer**: A

#### LO 8: Identify the combined tail area α as the total area in both tails of the sampling distribution used for the interval.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Combined Tail Area (Alpha)): The combined tail area α is the ______ in both tails of the distribution used for the confidence interval.
**Answer**: total area

#### LO 9: Calculate the tail area α/2 from a given confidence level or α.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Tail Area (Alpha/2)): If the combined tail area α is 0.10, what is the tail area α/2?
A. 0.05
B. 0.10
C. 0.90
D. 0.025
**Answer**: A

#### LO 10: Identify α/2 as the area in each tail of the sampling distribution for the interval.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Tail Area (Alpha/2)): α/2 is the ______ in each tail of the distribution used for the confidence interval.
**Answer**: area

#### LO 11: Define the random variable P′ as the sample proportion of surveyed students who were born in this state.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sample Proportion (P′)): P′ is the random variable representing the ______ for this survey.
**Answer**: sample proportion

#### LO 12: Calculate P′ using x and n from the class survey.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sample Proportion (P′)): In a class survey, x = 24 and n = 60. What is P′?
A. 0.40
B. 0.24
C. 0.60
D. 0.36
**Answer**: A

#### LO 13: State the sample size n for the class survey.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sample Size (n)): The sample size n is the ______ surveyed in the class.
**Answer**: number of students

#### LO 14: Use the sample size n when computing the sample proportion and the confidence interval.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sample Size (n)): Which of the following must be used when computing both the sample proportion and the confidence interval in this class survey?
A. Sample size n
B. Only α/2
C. Only x without n
D. A proportion from a different school
**Answer**: A

#### LO 15: Define x as the number of surveyed students who were born in this state.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Number Born In This State (x)): x is the ______ of surveyed students who were born in this state.
**Answer**: count

#### LO 16: Calculate x by counting the number of surveyed students born in this state.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Number Born In This State (x)): In a survey of 50 students, 18 were born in this state. What is x?
A. 18
B. 50
C. 0.36
D. 32
**Answer**: A

#### LO 17: State the estimated distribution to use for constructing the confidence interval for the sample proportion in this survey.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Estimated Distribution): The estimated distribution is the distribution stated for use when ______ the confidence interval for the sample proportion.
**Answer**: constructing

#### LO 18: Calculate the upper and lower limits of the confidence interval for the proportion.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Upper And Lower Limits): Given P′ = 0.55 and EBP = 0.03, what are the lower and upper limits of the confidence interval?
A. Lower 0.52, Upper 0.58
B. Lower 0.55, Upper 0.58
C. Lower 0.48, Upper 0.55
D. Lower 0.53, Upper 0.57
**Answer**: A

#### LO 19: Identify the upper limit, lower limit, and the sample proportion on a number line or diagram.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Upper And Lower Limits): A confidence interval is [0.30, 0.38] with P′ = 0.34. Which value is the upper limit?
A. 0.30
B. 0.34
C. 0.38
D. 0.32
**Answer**: C

#### LO 20: Interpret what a confidence interval means in general, using clear, nontechnical language.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Interpretation Of Confidence Interval): An interpretation of a confidence interval is the ______ of what a confidence interval means in general and for this particular study.
**Answer**: explanation

#### LO 21: Compare the error bounds and confidence interval widths obtained at 50%, 80%, 95%, and 99% confidence levels for the same sample.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Effect Of Confidence Level On Error Bound And Interval Width): As the confidence level increases from 80% to 95% for the same sample, what happens to the error bound and the width of the confidence interval?
A. Both increase
B. Both decrease
C. Error bound increases while width decreases
D. Error bound decreases while width increases
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_111

#### LO 1: Calculate a confidence interval for an unknown population parameter using the appropriate point estimate and error bound for a stated confidence level.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Confidence Interval (CI)): With point estimate x̄ and error bound EBM at a stated confidence level, which expression gives the confidence interval?
A. (x̄ − EBM, x̄ + EBM)
B. (x̄ − σ, x̄ + σ)
C. (μ − EBM, μ + EBM)
D. (x̄ − z, x̄ + z)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: Define confidence level as the percent of all possible samples for which the constructed interval contains the true population parameter.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Confidence Level (CL)): Fill in the blank: The confidence level is the _____ that a constructed confidence interval contains the true population parameter.
**Answer**: percent probability

#### LO 3: State the general form of a confidence interval as (point estimate − error bound, point estimate + error bound).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Confidence Interval General Form): Fill in the blank: The general form of a confidence interval is (lower bound, upper bound) = (_____).
**Answer**: point estimate − error bound, point estimate + error bound

#### LO 4: Explain how confidence level, sample size, and σ or s determine the size of the error bound.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Error Bound For A Population Mean (EBM)): Fill in the blank: The error bound for a population mean is the margin of error that depends on _____.
**Answer**: confidence level, sample size, and the known or estimated population standard deviation

#### LO 5: Define a point estimate as a single sample-based value (such as x̄ or p′) used to approximate a population parameter.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Point Estimate): Fill in the blank: A point estimate is _____ used to estimate a population parameter.
**Answer**: a single number computed from a sample

#### LO 6: Define standard deviation as the square root of the variance measuring typical distance from the mean, denoted s for a sample and σ for a population.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Standard Deviation): Fill in the blank: The standard deviation is the _____; denoted s for a sample and σ for a population.
**Answer**: square root of the variance measuring dispersion from the mean

#### LO 7: Define the normal distribution with parameters μ and σ and notation X ~ N(μ, σ).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Normal Distribution): Fill in the blank: A normal distribution is a continuous distribution with _____, denoted X ~ N(μ, σ).
**Answer**: mean μ and standard deviation σ

#### LO 8: Define the standard normal distribution as N(0, 1) and the associated z-scores.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Standard Normal Distribution): Fill in the blank: The standard normal distribution has _____.
**Answer**: mean 0 and standard deviation 1

#### LO 9: Define the critical z-value z(α/2) as the z-score with area α/2 to the right under the standard normal curve.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Critical Z-Value): Fill in the blank: The critical z-value is the z-score with area to the right equal to _____ under the standard normal curve.
**Answer**: α/2

#### LO 10: Define α as 1 − CL, representing the proportion of confidence intervals that will not contain the population parameter.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Alpha (α)): Fill in the blank: α is _____; α = 1 − CL.
**Answer**: the proportion of confidence intervals that do not contain the population parameter

#### LO 11: Define the sampling distribution of x̄ as (approximately) normal with mean μ and standard deviation σ/√n.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sampling Distribution Of The Sample Mean): Fill in the blank: The distribution of x̄ is normal with mean μ and standard deviation _____.
**Answer**: σ/√n

#### LO 12: Calculate a confidence interval for a single population mean when σ is known using EBM = z(α/2)·σ/√n and (x̄ − EBM, x̄ + EBM).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Confidence Interval For A Single Population Mean (σ Known)): When σ is known and normality applies, which formula and interval should you use to compute a CI for the population mean?
A. EBM = z(α/2)·σ/√n; CI = (x̄ − EBM, x̄ + EBM)
B. EBM = t(α/2, df)·s/√n; CI = (μ − EBM, μ + EBM)
C. EBM = z(α/2)·s/√n; CI = (x̄ − EBM, x̄ + EBM)
D. EBM = σ/√n; CI = (x̄ − z, x̄ + z)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 13: Explain that increasing the confidence level increases EBM and increasing the sample size decreases EBM.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Effect Of Confidence Level And Sample Size On Margin Of Error): Fill in the blank: As the confidence level increases, _____; as the sample size increases, _____.
**Answer**: EBM increases; EBM decreases
