## Section: seg_135

#### LO 1: describe a matched or paired-samples design in which two measurements are taken on the same pair and the analysis is based on their differences
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Matched Or Paired Samples): A matched or paired-samples design is one in which subjects are matched in pairs and two measurements are taken from the same pair; _____ are calculated for analysis.
**Answer**: differences between matched measurements

#### LO 2: calculate paired differences from matched measurements to prepare data for analysis
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Matched Or Paired Samples): To prepare matched measurements for analysis, what should you compute for each pair?
A. The paired difference for each pair
B. The combined total of all measurements
C. The average of all first measurements only
D. Treat the two measurements as independent observations
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: calculate the set of paired differences that constitute the sample used in the hypothesis test
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Differences As Data): In paired-samples analysis, the calculated _____ form the sample used in the hypothesis test.
**Answer**: paired differences

#### LO 4: state that the paired differences, not the original measurements, are the data analyzed in the test
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Differences As Data): In a paired-samples test, which data are analyzed?
A. The original measurements from each member of a pair
B. The paired differences
C. The group medians
D. The ratios of first to second measurements
**Answer**: B

#### LO 5: define μd as the population mean of the paired differences
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Population Mean Of Differences (μd)): μd is the parameter representing the _____ that is tested in the hypothesis.
**Answer**: mean of the paired differences

#### LO 6: interpret μd in context as the average change between paired measurements and the meaning of its sign
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Population Mean Of Differences (μd)): Which statement best interprets μd and the meaning of its sign?
A. It is the mean of the original measurements in the first group only.
B. It is the mean of the original measurements in the second group only.
C. It is the mean of the paired differences, representing average change between paired measurements; its sign indicates the direction of that change.
D. It is the variance of the paired differences.
**Answer**: C

#### LO 7: calculate the p-value for a paired t-test of μd using the t distribution with n − 1 degrees of freedom
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Paired t-Test For Mean Difference): To compute a p-value for testing μd with paired data, which distribution and degrees of freedom are used?
A. Normal distribution with n degrees of freedom
B. t distribution with n − 1 degrees of freedom
C. Chi-square distribution with n − 1 degrees of freedom
D. t distribution with 2n − 2 degrees of freedom
**Answer**: B

#### LO 8: state appropriate null and alternative hypotheses for μd for one-sided or two-sided questions
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Paired t-Test For Mean Difference): Which is a suitable set of hypotheses for a two-sided test about μd?
A. H0: μd = 0 vs HA: μd ≠ 0
B. H0: μd = μ1 − μ2 vs HA: μd ≠ μ1 − μ2
C. H0: μd > 0 vs HA: μd = 0
D. H0: μd = 1 vs HA: μd = −1
**Answer**: A

#### LO 9: interpret the p-value and decision rule to conclude whether to reject or not reject H0 at a stated significance level
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Paired t-Test For Mean Difference): A paired t-test yields p-value = 0.03 at α = 0.05. What is the decision?
A. Reject H0
B. Do not reject H0
C. Increase the sample size
D. Conclude μd = 0 exactly
**Answer**: A

#### LO 10: state the formula t = (x̄d − μd) / (sd/√n) for the paired t-test statistic
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Test Statistic For Paired t-Test): Which formula gives the test statistic for the paired t-test?
A. t = (x̄ − μ) / (s/√n)
B. t = (x̄d − μd) / (sd/√n)
C. z = (x̄d − μd) / (sd/√n)
D. t = (μd − x̄d) / sd
**Answer**: B

#### LO 11: calculate the t-score given x̄d, sd, n, and a hypothesized μd
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Test Statistic For Paired t-Test): Given x̄d = 1.2, sd = 2.0, n = 25, and μd = 0, what is the paired t-test statistic?
A. 3.0
B. 2.5
C. 0.6
D. −3.0
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: list the assumptions for a paired-samples t-test, including simple random sampling, small sample sizes, and normality of differences or sufficiently large n for approximate normality
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Assumptions For Paired Samples Test): One assumption for a paired-samples t-test is that the differences come from a _____ or that n is sufficiently large for approximate normality.
**Answer**: normal population

#### LO 13: define X̄d as the sample mean of the paired differences used in the t test
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Random Variable X̄d): X̄d is the _____ used in the t test.
**Answer**: sample mean of the paired differences

#### LO 14: calculate X̄d from a set of paired differences
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Random Variable X̄d): Given paired differences 2, −1, 3, 0, and 4, what is X̄d?
A. 1.6
B. 1.4
C. 2.0
D. 0.8
**Answer**: A

#### LO 15: define the degrees of freedom for a paired t test as n − 1, where n is the number of paired differences
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom For Paired t-Test): The degrees of freedom for a paired t-test are _____, where n is the number of paired differences.
**Answer**: n − 1

#### LO 16: calculate the degrees of freedom for a paired t test given n
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom For Paired t-Test): For n = 12 paired differences, what are the degrees of freedom?
A. 12
B. 11
C. 10
D. 24
**Answer**: B


---
## Section: seg_137

#### LO 1: identify when to use a two-sample t-test for independent means with unknown population standard deviations and the associated Student’s t-distribution without pooling variances
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Two Population Means With Unknown Standard Deviations): Which test applies when comparing two population means from independent samples with unknown population standard deviations?
A. Two-sample t-test using Student’s t-distribution with degrees of freedom computed without pooling variances
B. Two-sample z-test using the normal distribution with known population standard deviations
C. Matched-pairs t-test on paired differences with n − 1 degrees of freedom
D. Two-proportion z-test using the normal distribution for the difference in estimated proportions
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: calculate the t-test statistic, appropriate degrees of freedom, standard error, and p-value for comparing two population means with unknown standard deviations using sample data
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Two Population Means With Unknown Standard Deviations): Which set of components is required to carry out this two-sample test with unknown standard deviations?
A. t-statistic, standard error, p-value, and Student’s t-distribution with degrees of freedom computed without pooling variances
B. z-statistic, standard error, p-value, and the normal distribution with known standard deviations
C. t-statistic with pooled variances and a normal distribution
D. Only the difference of sample means without a test statistic
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: interpret the decision and conclusion from a two-sample t-test with unknown standard deviations in the context of the problem
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Two Population Means With Unknown Standard Deviations): A correct conclusion from this test should address whether there is a difference between:
A. the two population means being compared from independent samples
B. a single population mean and a specified value
C. two population proportions
D. the mean of paired differences and zero
**Answer**: A

#### LO 4: identify situations requiring a two-sample z-test for independent means with known population standard deviations and the normal distribution
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Two Population Means With Known Standard Deviations): Which situation calls for a two-sample z-test for means?
A. Comparing two population means from independent samples when population standard deviations are known (or approximated); use the normal distribution
B. Comparing two population means from independent samples when population standard deviations are unknown; use Student’s t-distribution
C. Comparing two measurements on the same objects; use a paired t-test
D. Comparing two population proportions using the normal distribution
**Answer**: A

#### LO 5: calculate the z-test statistic, standard error, and p-value for comparing two population means when population standard deviations are known or approximated by sample standard deviations
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Two Population Means With Known Standard Deviations): To carry out this two-sample z-test for means, which elements are needed?
A. z-statistic, standard error, p-value, and the normal distribution
B. t-statistic, degrees of freedom, and Student’s t-distribution
C. Mean of paired differences and n − 1 degrees of freedom
D. Pooled proportion and a z-statistic for proportions
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: interpret the outcomes of a two-sample z-test for means, stating the decision and a clear conclusion about the difference in population means
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Two Population Means With Known Standard Deviations): A correct conclusion from a two-sample z-test for means should focus on:
A. whether the two population means differ
B. whether a single population mean equals a target value
C. whether two population proportions differ
D. whether the mean of paired differences equals zero
**Answer**: A

#### LO 7: identify when to compare two independent population proportions and that the sampling distribution of p-hat differences is approximately normal
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Comparing Two Independent Population Proportions): Which description matches comparing two independent population proportions?
A. Two independent samples with interest in the difference of estimated proportions, using the normal distribution
B. Two independent samples comparing means with known standard deviations, using the normal distribution
C. Two measurements on the same objects, testing mean paired differences with Student’s t
D. Two independent samples comparing means with unknown standard deviations, using Student’s t
**Answer**: A

#### LO 8: calculate the pooled proportion, standard error, z-test statistic, and p-value for a two-proportion hypothesis test
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Comparing Two Independent Population Proportions): Which components are needed to conduct a two-proportion hypothesis test?
A. Pooled proportion, standard error, z-test statistic, and p-value
B. Pooled variance, t-test statistic, and degrees of freedom
C. Mean of paired differences and Student’s t-distribution
D. Known population standard deviations and a z-test for means
**Answer**: A

#### LO 9: interpret the results of a two-proportion z-test in context, including the direction and meaning of any significant difference
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Comparing Two Independent Population Proportions): A correct interpretation of a significant two-proportion z-test should state:
A. which population proportion is larger and what that difference means in context
B. that a single population proportion equals a target value
C. that two population means differ
D. that the mean of paired differences equals zero
**Answer**: A

#### LO 10: classify a study as matched or paired samples versus independent samples based on whether two measurements are taken on the same objects
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Matched Or Paired Samples): When is a study classified as matched or paired samples?
A. When two measurements are taken on the same objects and the test targets the mean of the paired differences
B. When two measurements are taken on different objects and variances are not pooled
C. When comparing two population proportions using the normal distribution
D. When comparing two independent means with known standard deviations using the normal distribution
**Answer**: A

#### LO 11: calculate the paired differences, mean difference, t-test statistic with n − 1 degrees of freedom, and p-value for a matched-pairs t-test assuming normal differences when the number of pairs is small
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Matched Or Paired Samples): Which steps/components belong to a matched-pairs t-test?
A. Compute paired differences, the mean difference, a t-test statistic with n − 1 degrees of freedom, and a p-value
B. Compute a pooled proportion, a z-test statistic, and a p-value
C. Compute a z-test statistic for two means with known standard deviations
D. Compute a t-statistic for two independent samples with pooled variances
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: define degrees of freedom as the number of values in a sample that are free to vary
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom (Df)): Degrees of freedom are the number of objects in a sample that are ____.
**Answer**: free to vary

#### LO 13: calculate the degrees of freedom for a paired t-test as n − 1 and for a two-sample t-test with unknown, unequal variances using the provided formula
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom (Df)): Which statement about degrees of freedom is correct for these tests?
A. Paired t-test: df = n − 1; Two-sample t-test with unknown, unequal variances: df is computed using a formula
B. Paired t-test: df is computed using a formula; Two-sample test: df = n − 1
C. Both tests use the normal distribution and do not require degrees of freedom
D. Both tests use df based only on known population standard deviations
**Answer**: A

#### LO 14: define the pooled proportion as the estimated common population proportion used when testing two proportions
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Pooled Proportion): The pooled proportion is an estimate of the ____ population proportion when testing two proportions.
**Answer**: common

#### LO 15: calculate the pooled proportion from sample successes and sizes using pc = (x1 + x2) / (n1 + n2)
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Pooled Proportion): Which expression gives the pooled proportion pc?
A. pc = (x1 + x2) / (n1 + n2)
B. pc = x1/n1 − x2/n2
C. pc = (x1/n1 + x2/n2) / 2
D. pc = (x1 − x2) / (n1 + n2)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 16: define standard deviation as the square root of the variance that measures how far data values are from their mean, denoted s for a sample and σ for a population
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Standard Deviation): Standard deviation is the ____ that measures how far data values are from their mean; denoted s for a sample and σ for a population.
**Answer**: square root of the variance

#### LO 17: use standard deviation values to compute the standard error for the difference of two means in hypothesis tests
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Standard Deviation): Why are standard deviations used when forming the standard error for the difference of two means?
A. They measure how far data values are from their mean and provide inputs to the standard error for the difference of two means
B. They estimate a common population proportion for two samples
C. They directly determine the degrees of freedom
D. They classify samples as paired or independent
**Answer**: A

#### LO 18: define a random variable as a characteristic of interest whose possible values may be numeric or categorical and are known only after the experiment
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Variable (Random Variable)): A random variable is a ____ in a population; its realized value is known only after performing the experiment.
**Answer**: characteristic of interest

#### LO 19: describe the random variables used in this section, such as X-bar1 − X-bar2, p-hatA − p-hatB, and d, including their notation and what they represent in context
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Variable (Random Variable)): Which statement best describes X-bar1 − X-bar2, p-hatA − p-hatB, and d in this section?
A. They are random variables representing characteristics of interest (differences like two sample means, two estimated proportions, and paired differences) whose realized values are known only after the experiment
B. They are fixed population parameters that do not vary from sample to sample
C. They are standard deviations used to measure spread around the mean
D. They are degrees of freedom used to select a distribution
**Answer**: A
