## Section: seg_141

#### LO 1: state the notation for the chi-square distribution as X ~ χ2 with df degrees of freedom and that the random variable symbol may be any uppercase letter.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Chi-Square Distribution Notation): The chi-square distribution is denoted ____ with df degrees of freedom; the random variable symbol is often χ2 but may be any uppercase letter.
**Answer**: X ~ χ2

#### LO 2: define degrees of freedom (df) for the chi-square distribution as the parameter that depends on how chi-square is used, with df = n − 1 for practice.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom): In chi-square applications, df represents degrees of freedom and depends on how it is used; for practice, ____.
**Answer**: df = n − 1

#### LO 3: describe how degrees of freedom depend on the chi-square application and that the three major uses calculate df differently, with df = n − 1 for practice.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom): Which statement best describes degrees of freedom (df) in chi-square problems?
A. They depend on the chi-square application; the three major uses calculate df differently, and for practice df = n − 1.
B. They are always equal to the sample size n.
C. They are fixed at df = 1 for all chi-square uses.
D. They must be negative for right-skewed curves.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 4: define the population mean of a chi-square distribution as equal to its degrees of freedom (μ = df).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Chi-Square Mean): For the χ2 distribution, the population mean equals the ____.
**Answer**: degrees of freedom

#### LO 5: calculate the population mean of a chi-square distribution from a given degrees of freedom using μ = df.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Mean): If a chi-square distribution has df = 12, what is its population mean μ?
A. 10
B. 12
C. 24
D. 0
**Answer**: B

#### LO 6: define a chi-square random variable with k degrees of freedom as the sum of k independent, squared standard normal variables.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sum Of Squared Standard Normals): A χ2 random variable with k degrees of freedom is defined as which of the following?
A. The sum of k independent, squared standard normal variables.
B. The sum of k independent standard normal variables (not squared).
C. The product of k squared standard normal variables.
D. The difference of two standard normal variables.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 7: describe the chi-square distribution as nonsymmetrical and skewed to the right.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Distribution Shape): The chi-square distribution is ____.
**Answer**: nonsymmetrical and skewed to the right

#### LO 8: recognize that the chi-square curve is nonsymmetrical and skewed to the right.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Distribution Shape): Which description best matches the shape of the chi-square curve?
A. Symmetric and bell-shaped.
B. Uniform across its range.
C. Nonsymmetrical and skewed to the right.
D. Symmetric and skewed to the left.
**Answer**: C

#### LO 9: define the family of chi-square distributions as having a different curve for each value of degrees of freedom.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Family Of Chi-Square Curves): There is a different chi-square curve for ____.
**Answer**: each value of df

#### LO 10: state that the chi-square test statistic is always greater than or equal to zero.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Nonnegativity Of Test Statistic): Which statement about the chi-square test statistic is correct?
A. It is always greater than or equal to zero.
B. It can be negative when sample sizes are small.
C. It is always strictly positive (never zero).
D. It can take any real-number value.
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_143

#### LO 1: calculate the chi-square goodness-of-fit test statistic from observed and expected frequencies to assess fit to a specified distribution.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Goodness-Of-Fit Test): Given O = [18, 22, 10] and E = [20, 20, 10], what is the chi-square test statistic?
A. 0.4
B. 0.2
C. 0.8
D. 2.0
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: state the null and alternative hypotheses for a goodness-of-fit test in words or symbols.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Goodness-Of-Fit Test): In a goodness-of-fit test, H0 states the data ________; Ha states the data do not.
**Answer**: fit the specified distribution

#### LO 3: interpret the outcome of a goodness-of-fit test in context using the p-value and a chosen significance level.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Goodness-Of-Fit Test): If the p-value is 0.03 and α = 0.05, what is the decision and conclusion?
A. Reject H0; conclude the data do not fit the specified distribution.
B. Reject H0; conclude the data fit the specified distribution.
C. Do not reject H0; conclude the data do not fit the specified distribution.
D. Do not reject H0; conclude the data fit the specified distribution.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 4: define the chi-square test statistic as the sum across categories of (O − E)² divided by E.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Chi-Square Test Statistic): The chi-square test statistic is the sum across categories of ________.
**Answer**: (O − E)² / E

#### LO 5: calculate the chi-square test statistic from a table of observed and expected counts.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Test Statistic): Given O = [9, 11, 10, 10] and E = [10, 10, 10, 10], what is the chi-square statistic?
A. 0.2
B. 0.4
C. 2.0
D. 1.0
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: describe how larger differences between observed and expected counts increase the chi-square statistic and move results into the right tail.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Test Statistic): E = [10, 10, 10]. Scenario 1: O = [9, 11, 10]. Scenario 2: O = [5, 15, 10]. Which scenario yields a larger chi-square and lies farther in the right tail?
A. Scenario 2, because larger |O − E| increases the chi-square and moves it to the right tail.
B. Scenario 1, because smaller |O − E| increases the chi-square and moves it to the right tail.
C. Both scenarios yield the same chi-square and tail position.
D. Neither scenario; the chi-square does not depend on |O − E|.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 7: define observed values as the recorded sample frequencies for each category.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Observed Values (O)): Observed values are the ________ for each category.
**Answer**: data values recorded

#### LO 8: use observed values with expected counts to compute (O − E)²/E for each category.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Observed Values (O)): For a category with O = 13 and E = 10, what is (O − E)²/E?
A. 0.9
B. 3.0
C. 0.3
D. -0.9
**Answer**: A

#### LO 9: define expected values as the category frequencies predicted under the null hypothesis.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Expected Values (E)): Expected values are the ________ in each category if the null hypothesis is true.
**Answer**: frequencies expected

#### LO 10: calculate expected frequencies from a specified distribution and sample size for each category.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Expected Values (E)): With n = 80 and a specified distribution where P(Category B) = 0.25, what is the expected frequency for Category B?
A. 20
B. 25
C. 15
D. 0.25
**Answer**: A

#### LO 11: calculate the degrees of freedom for a goodness-of-fit test as k − 1, where k is the number of categories.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom): For k = 6 categories, what is the degrees of freedom for the goodness-of-fit test?
A. 5
B. 6
C. 4
D. k − 2
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: state the degrees of freedom for a given number of categories as k − 1.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom): For k categories, the degrees of freedom is df = ________.
**Answer**: k − 1

#### LO 13: state that the chi-square goodness-of-fit test is almost always right-tailed.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Right-Tailed Test): The chi-square goodness-of-fit test is almost always ________.
**Answer**: right-tailed

#### LO 14: explain why the test is right-tailed by linking large sums of (O − E)²/E to the right tail of the chi-square distribution.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Right-Tailed Test): Why is the chi-square goodness-of-fit test right-tailed?
A. Because larger sums of (O − E)²/E indicate larger discrepancies and fall in the right tail of the chi-square distribution.
B. Because the chi-square statistic can be negative, so only the right tail is valid.
C. Because small discrepancies push the statistic into the right tail.
D. Because the left tail is used only when discrepancies are very large.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 15: calculate the right-tail probability P(χ² > test statistic) for the appropriate degrees of freedom.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Right-Tailed Test): Which expression represents the right-tail probability for a chi-square test statistic x with d degrees of freedom?
A. P(χ²_d > x)
B. P(χ²_d < x)
C. P(t_d > x)
D. P(χ²_(d−1) > x)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 16: state the requirement that each expected cell frequency should be at least five to validly use the chi-square goodness-of-fit test.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Expected Count Requirement): Each expected cell frequency must be at least ________ to validly use the test.
**Answer**: five

#### LO 17: construct combined categories to ensure each expected frequency is at least five before performing the test.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Expected Count Requirement): If some expected category counts are below 5, what should you do before performing the test?
A. Combine categories with small expected counts so each combined expected count is at least five.
B. Proceed with the test as-is; the requirement is optional.
C. Delete categories with expected counts below five.
D. Increase expected counts by duplicating observations.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 18: state H0 that the data fit the specified distribution and Ha that they do not.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Hypotheses For Goodness-Of-Fit): In a goodness-of-fit test, H0 states the data fit the specified distribution; Ha states the data ________.
**Answer**: do not fit the specified distribution

#### LO 19: state that the test statistic follows a chi-square distribution with degrees of freedom equal to k − 1.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Test Distribution): The test statistic follows the ________ with degrees of freedom equal to k − 1.
**Answer**: chi-square distribution

#### LO 20: calculate the degrees of freedom to determine the appropriate chi-square distribution for a given table of categories.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Test Distribution): A goodness-of-fit test with four categories uses which test distribution?
A. Chi-square distribution with df = 3
B. Standard normal distribution
C. t-distribution with df = 3
D. Chi-square distribution with df = 4
**Answer**: A

#### LO 21: define the p-value as the right-tail probability P(χ² > calculated statistic) under the chi-square distribution with the appropriate degrees of freedom.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: P-Value For Goodness-Of-Fit): For a goodness-of-fit test, the p-value is the ________ P(χ² > calculated statistic) under the chi-square distribution with the appropriate degrees of freedom.
**Answer**: right-tail probability

#### LO 22: calculate the p-value for a goodness-of-fit test using technology or chi-square cumulative distribution functions.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: P-Value For Goodness-Of-Fit): Given a test statistic x = 7.5 with df = 2, which probability equals the p-value?
A. P(χ²_2 > 7.5)
B. P(χ²_2 < 7.5)
C. P(t_2 > 7.5)
D. P(χ²_3 > 7.5)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 23: interpret the p-value to decide whether to reject or not reject H0 at a stated significance level in context.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: P-Value For Goodness-Of-Fit): If the p-value is 0.12 and α = 0.05, what is the decision and conclusion?
A. Reject H0; conclude the data do not fit the specified distribution.
B. Do not reject H0; there is insufficient evidence that the data do not fit the specified distribution.
C. Reject H0; conclude the data fit the specified distribution.
D. Do not reject H0; conclude the data fit the specified distribution with certainty.
**Answer**: B


---
## Section: seg_145

#### LO 1: State that a chi-square test of independence uses a contingency table to assess whether two categorical factors are independent.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Test Of Independence): A chi-square test of independence uses a contingency table of observed frequencies to determine whether two factors are ____.
**Answer**: independent

#### LO 2: Identify rows, columns, and cell entries in a contingency table as observed frequencies for two categorical factors.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Contingency Table): In a contingency table, what do the numbers in the interior cells represent?
A. Observed frequencies (data counts for each category combination).
B. Expected frequencies computed under independence.
C. Probabilities P(A AND B).
D. The p-value of the test.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: Calculate row totals, column totals, and the overall sample size from a contingency table.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Contingency Table): Given this contingency table of counts: Row A: 5, 7, 8; Row B: 6, 9, 5. What is the overall sample size?
A. 34
B. 40
C. 36
D. 20
**Answer**: B

#### LO 4: Define an observed frequency as the data count recorded in a specific cell of a contingency table.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Observed Frequency (O)): An observed frequency is the data count recorded in a ____ of the contingency table.
**Answer**: cell

#### LO 5: Differentiate observed frequencies (O) from expected frequencies (E) used under the independence assumption.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Observed Frequency (O)): Which statement correctly differentiates O and E in a test of independence?
A. O are data counts in a cell; E are counts expected under independence computed as (row total × column total) ÷ total surveyed.
B. O are counts expected under independence; E are the observed data in a cell.
C. O and E must always be equal in every cell.
D. O are probabilities; E are p-values.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: Calculate an expected frequency for any cell using (row total × column total) ÷ total surveyed and check that it is at least five.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Expected Frequency (E)): In a 2×2 table with Row1 total 30, Row2 total 20, Column1 total 25, Column2 total 25, and total surveyed 50, what is the expected frequency for the cell at Row1–Column1?
A. 15
B. 12.5
C. 10
D. 7.5
**Answer**: A

#### LO 7: State that each expected cell count should be at least five to use the chi-square test of independence.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Expected Frequency (E)): Each expected cell count should be at least ____ to use the chi-square test of independence.
**Answer**: five

#### LO 8: Define the chi-square test statistic for independence as the sum over all cells of (O − E)² ÷ E.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Chi-Square Test Statistic): The chi-square test statistic for independence is the sum over all cells of ____.
**Answer**: (O − E)² ÷ E

#### LO 9: Calculate the chi-square test statistic by summing (O − E)² ÷ E across all i × j cells of the table.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Test Statistic): Consider this 2×2 table (row totals 30 and 20; column totals 25 and 25; total 50): Row1: (18, 12); Row2: (7, 13). What is the chi-square test statistic Σ (O − E)²/E?
A. 0.0
B. 1.5
C. 3.0
D. 6.0
**Answer**: C

#### LO 10: Interpret larger chi-square values as indicating greater discrepancy between observed and expected counts and movement further into the right tail.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Test Statistic): What does a larger chi-square value indicate in a test of independence?
A. Greater discrepancy between observed and expected counts and movement further into the right tail.
B. Smaller discrepancy and movement toward the left tail.
C. That the two factors are independent.
D. That expected counts are less than five.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 11: Calculate the degrees of freedom for a test of independence as (number of columns − 1) × (number of rows − 1).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom For Independence Test): For a 3×4 contingency table (3 rows, 4 columns), what are the degrees of freedom for the independence test?
A. 6
B. 12
C. 3
D. 8
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: State the formula for the degrees of freedom of a chi-square test of independence as (c − 1)(r − 1).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom For Independence Test): The degrees of freedom for a chi-square test of independence are ____.
**Answer**: (Number of columns − 1) × (Number of rows − 1)

#### LO 13: State the null hypothesis that the two factors are independent and the alternative hypothesis that they are not independent (dependent).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Hypotheses For Independence Test): The null hypothesis for a chi-square test of independence states the two factors are ____.
**Answer**: independent

#### LO 14: State that the chi-square test of independence is always right-tailed.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Right-Tailed Test): The chi-square test of independence is always ____-tailed.
**Answer**: right

#### LO 15: Explain that the test is right-tailed because larger differences between observed and expected counts produce larger chi-square statistics in the right tail of the distribution.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Right-Tailed Test): Why is the chi-square test of independence right-tailed?
A. Larger differences between observed and expected values produce larger statistics in the right tail of the chi-square distribution.
B. The chi-square distribution is symmetric.
C. Negative discrepancies accumulate in the left tail.
D. Because degrees of freedom are always one.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 16: Define independence of events A and B by stating P(A AND B) = P(A)P(B).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Independence Of Events): Two events A and B are independent if ____.
**Answer**: P(A AND B) = P(A)P(B)

#### LO 17: Calculate an expected joint probability or count under independence using P(A AND B) = P(A)P(B) and sample totals.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Independence Of Events): In a sample of 200 with P(A)=0.40 and P(B)=0.30, what is the expected joint count under independence?
A. 24
B. 60
C. 80
D. 12
**Answer**: A

#### LO 18: Calculate the p-value for a test of independence as the right-tail probability P(χ² > observed χ²) with the appropriate degrees of freedom, using technology.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: P-Value For Independence Test): Given an observed chi-square statistic of 5.2 with the appropriate degrees of freedom, the p-value for the test of independence is which probability?
A. P(χ² > 5.2) under the chi-square distribution with those degrees of freedom.
B. P(χ² < 5.2) with those degrees of freedom.
C. P(χ² > degrees of freedom).
D. P(χ² = 5.2).
**Answer**: A

#### LO 19: Interpret the p-value by comparing it to a significance level α to decide whether to reject or fail to reject the null hypothesis of independence.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: P-Value For Independence Test): At significance level α = 0.05, the test yields p-value = 0.08. What is the decision?
A. Reject H0 (independence) and conclude the factors are dependent.
B. Fail to reject H0 (independence); insufficient evidence of dependence.
C. Reject H0 and conclude the factors are independent.
D. Fail to reject H0; conclude the factors are dependent.
**Answer**: B

#### LO 20: State that the p-value for a chi-square test of independence is P(χ² > observed test statistic) under the chi-square distribution with the test’s degrees of freedom.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: P-Value For Independence Test): The p-value for a chi-square test of independence is ____ under the chi-square distribution with the test’s degrees of freedom.
**Answer**: P(χ² > observed test statistic)

#### LO 21: Interpret the test statistic and p-value to decide whether two factors are independent or dependent at a stated significance level.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Test Of Independence): At α = 0.05, a chi-square test of independence yields a p-value of 0.012. How should you interpret this?
A. Reject the null; conclude the factors are dependent.
B. Fail to reject the null; conclude the factors are independent.
C. Reject the null; conclude the factors are independent.
D. Fail to reject the null; no conclusion can be drawn.
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_147

#### LO 1: State that the test for homogeneity is used to decide whether two populations have the same distribution.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Test For Homogeneity): The test for homogeneity is used to determine whether ____.
**Answer**: two populations have the same distribution

#### LO 2: Calculate the chi-square test statistic for a test for homogeneity using the same procedure as the test for independence.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Test For Homogeneity): How is the test statistic computed for a test for homogeneity?
A. In the same way as in the test of independence.
B. In a different way than in the test of independence.
C. It varies with the populations and is not fixed.
D. No statistic is calculated for this test.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: State the null hypothesis that the distributions of the two populations are the same.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Null Hypothesis): In a test for homogeneity, the null hypothesis states that the distributions of the two populations _____.
**Answer**: are the same

#### LO 4: Define the alternative hypothesis as the distributions of the two populations are not the same.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Alternative Hypothesis): In a test for homogeneity, the alternative hypothesis states that the distributions of the two populations _____.
**Answer**: are not the same

#### LO 5: Calculate the χ2 test statistic for a homogeneity test in the same way as for a test of independence.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Test Statistic): Which test statistic is used for a homogeneity test, and how is it computed?
A. A χ2 test statistic computed in the same way as the test for independence.
B. A χ2 test statistic computed differently than the test for independence.
C. A non-χ2 statistic.
D. No test statistic is used.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: State that a χ2 test statistic is used for the test for homogeneity.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Test Statistic): For a test for homogeneity, the test statistic used is ____.
**Answer**: a χ2 test statistic

#### LO 7: State that the degrees of freedom for a homogeneity test equal the number of columns minus one.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom): For a homogeneity test, the degrees of freedom formula is df = _____.
**Answer**: number of columns − 1

#### LO 8: Calculate the degrees of freedom for a given contingency table as the number of columns minus one.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom): A contingency table has 5 columns. What is the degrees of freedom for the homogeneity test?
A. 4
B. 5
C. 6
D. 3
**Answer**: A

#### LO 9: Define the expected cell frequency condition that each expected count must be at least five to use the test for homogeneity.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Expected Cell Frequency Condition): To use the test for homogeneity, the expected value for each cell must be at least ____.
**Answer**: five

#### LO 10: State common uses of the test for homogeneity, such as comparing men vs. women, before vs. after, or east vs. west.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Common Uses): Which is a common use of the test for homogeneity?
A. Comparing two populations.
B. Showing how distributions differ.
C. Analyzing a variable with only two response values.
D. Estimating expected cell frequencies.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 11: Define the applicable variable type as a categorical variable with more than two possible response values.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Applicable Variable Type): The applicable variable type is _____ with more than two possible response values.
**Answer**: categorical

#### LO 12: State that the test for homogeneity only determines whether distributions are the same or not and does not show how they differ.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Interpretation Limitation): The test indicates whether distributions are the same or not but does not show _____.
**Answer**: how they differ


---
## Section: seg_149

#### LO 1: Identify when the chi-square goodness-of-fit test is appropriate: a single qualitative variable from one population to assess fit to a known distribution with H0 that the population fits and Ha that it does not.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Chi-Square Goodness-Of-Fit Test): The chi-square goodness-of-fit test is used to decide whether a population with an unknown distribution ______; it applies to a single qualitative question from one population (H0: the population fits; Ha: it does not).
**Answer**: fits a known distribution

#### LO 2: Identify situations requiring a chi-square test of independence: two qualitative variables summarized in a contingency table to test H0 that the variables are independent versus Ha that they are dependent.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Test Of Independence): Which situation calls for a chi-square test of independence?
A. One qualitative variable from one population to assess fit to a known distribution.
B. Two qualitative variables summarized in a contingency table to test whether they are independent.
C. The same qualitative question posed to two populations to test whether they have the same distribution.
D. Two qualitative variables from two different populations to test whether the populations have the same distribution.
**Answer**: B

#### LO 3: Describe the purpose and setup of the chi-square test of independence, including two qualitative variables, use of a contingency table, and the null and alternative hypotheses.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Chi-Square Test Of Independence): In the chi-square test of independence, H0 states that the two variables are ______, and Ha states that they are dependent; the setup involves two qualitative variables in a contingency table.
**Answer**: independent

#### LO 4: Identify when the chi-square test of homogeneity is appropriate: the same qualitative question posed to two populations to assess whether they share the same distribution (H0) or have different distributions (Ha).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Test Of Homogeneity): Which study design is appropriate for the chi-square test of homogeneity?
A. One qualitative question given to a single population to assess fit to a known distribution.
B. Two qualitative variables from one population arranged in a contingency table to test independence.
C. The same qualitative question administered to two populations to test whether they follow the same distribution.
D. Two different qualitative questions asked of one population to test whether they are dependent.
**Answer**: C

#### LO 5: Differentiate the chi-square test of homogeneity from the goodness-of-fit and independence tests by the study designs they address (two populations with one question versus one population with one question versus two variables in a contingency table) and their hypotheses.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Test Of Homogeneity): Which pairing correctly differentiates the three chi-square tests by study design and hypotheses?
A. Goodness-of-fit: one population, one qualitative variable vs known distribution (H0: fits); Independence: two qualitative variables in a contingency table (H0: independent); Homogeneity: same qualitative question across two populations (H0: same distribution).
B. Goodness-of-fit: two populations with one question (H0: same distribution); Independence: one population, one qualitative variable vs known distribution; Homogeneity: two variables in a contingency table (H0: independent).
C. Goodness-of-fit: two variables in a contingency table (H0: independent); Independence: two populations with one question (H0: same distribution); Homogeneity: one population, one qualitative variable vs known distribution.
D. Goodness-of-fit: one population, one qualitative variable to test independence; Independence: one qualitative variable vs known distribution; Homogeneity: two populations with one qualitative question (Ha: same distribution).
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_151

#### LO 1: state the normality assumption underlying a test of a single population variance.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Test Of A Single Variance): A test of a single population variance assumes the underlying distribution is ____.
**Answer**: normal

#### LO 2: compute the p-value for a single-variance hypothesis test using the χ² distribution with df = n − 1.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Test Of A Single Variance): To compute the p-value for a single-variance test, which distribution and degrees of freedom should you use?
A. χ² with df = n − 1
B. χ² with df = n
C. χ² with df = n + 1
D. χ² with df = 2n − 1
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: compare right-, left-, and two-tailed versions of the single-variance test based on the direction of the claim.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Test Of A Single Variance): If the claim is σ² > σ0², which version of the single-variance test applies?
A. Right-tailed
B. Left-tailed
C. Two-tailed
D. Not applicable
**Answer**: A

#### LO 4: define the null and alternative hypotheses in terms of σ² (or σ) for a claim about variability.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Null And Alternative Hypotheses For Single Variance Test): In a single-variance test, the null and alternative hypotheses are written in terms of the ____.
**Answer**: population variance σ² (or the population standard deviation σ)

#### LO 5: formulate H0 and Ha for single-variance tests from verbal claims such as less than, greater than, or equal to a stated standard deviation.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Null And Alternative Hypotheses For Single Variance Test): A claim states that the population standard deviation is less than a stated value σ0. Which formulation is correct?
A. H0: σ ≥ σ0; Ha: σ < σ0
B. H0: σ ≤ σ0; Ha: σ > σ0
C. H0: σ = σ0; Ha: σ ≠ σ0
D. H0: σ² < σ0²; Ha: σ² ≥ σ0²
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: state that the test statistic is (n − 1)s²/σ² for testing a single variance.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Test Statistic For Single Variance): The test statistic for a single-variance test is ____.
**Answer**: (n − 1)s²/σ²

#### LO 7: calculate the chi-square test statistic (n − 1)s²/σ² from a given sample size, sample standard deviation, and hypothesized variance.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Test Statistic For Single Variance): Given n = 6, s = 2, and hypothesized variance σ0² = 4, what is the chi-square test statistic?
A. 5
B. 4
C. 2.5
D. 1
**Answer**: A

#### LO 8: define the degrees of freedom for the chi-square test of a single variance as df = n − 1.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom): For the chi-square test of a single variance, the degrees of freedom are ____.
**Answer**: df = n − 1

#### LO 9: compute the degrees of freedom for a sample of size n using df = n − 1.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom): For a sample of size n = 12, what are the degrees of freedom for the test?
A. 11
B. 12
C. 10
D. 13
**Answer**: A

#### LO 10: state that the test statistic follows a χ² distribution with df = n − 1 under the null hypothesis.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Chi-Square Distribution For Single Variance Test): Under the null hypothesis, the sampling distribution of the test statistic is ____ with df = n − 1.
**Answer**: χ²

#### LO 11: compute a left-, right-, or two-tailed χ² p-value using χ2cdf with appropriate bounds and degrees of freedom.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Distribution For Single Variance Test): For a right-tailed chi-square test with test statistic x0 and df = n − 1, which expression gives the p-value?
A. P(χ² ≥ x0)
B. P(χ² ≤ x0)
C. 2P(χ² ≥ x0)
D. 1 − P(χ² ≥ x0)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: define the random variable for the test as the sample standard deviation s.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Random Variable In Single Variance Test): In the single-variance test, the random variable is the ____.
**Answer**: sample standard deviation s

#### LO 13: identify whether the test is right-tailed, left-tailed, or two-tailed from the wording of the claim.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Tail Types For Single Variance Test): Claim: σ ≠ σ0. What type of chi-square test should be used?
A. Right-tailed
B. Left-tailed
C. Two-tailed
D. Neither
**Answer**: C

#### LO 14: define s² as the sample variance used in the chi-square test statistic.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sample Variance (s²)): s² is ____.
**Answer**: the variance computed from the sample

#### LO 15: differentiate between s² (sample variance) and σ² (population variance) in setting up hypotheses and the test statistic.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sample Variance (s²)): Which statement correctly distinguishes s² and σ² in a single-variance test?
A. s² is the sample variance used in the test statistic; σ² is the population variance referenced in H0 and Ha.
B. s² is the population variance; σ² is the sample variance used in the test statistic.
C. Both s² and σ² are sample quantities computed from the data.
D. Both s² and σ² are population parameters stated in the hypotheses.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 16: use the sample variance or standard deviation to compute the test statistic and p-value for a single-variance test.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sample Variance (s²)): To compute the chi-square test statistic (n − 1)s²/σ², which quantities must you use?
A. The sample variance s² and the hypothesized population variance σ².
B. The sample standard deviation s and the sample variance s².
C. The hypothesized population standard deviation σ and the sample mean.
D. Only the hypothesized population variance σ².
**Answer**: A

#### LO 17: state that hypotheses for the single-variance test are written in terms of the population variance σ² (or standard deviation σ).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Population Variance (σ²)): In the single-variance test, hypotheses are written in terms of the ____.
**Answer**: population variance σ² (or standard deviation σ)

#### LO 18: calculate the hypothesized variance σ² by squaring a stated standard deviation when writing H0 and Ha.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Population Variance (σ²)): A stated standard deviation is σ0 = 5. What is the hypothesized variance σ0² used in H0 and Ha?
A. 25
B. 5
C. 10
D. 2.5
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_153

#### LO 1: calculate the chi-square goodness-of-fit test statistic using observed and expected frequencies for a uniform or exponential model
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Goodness-Of-Fit Test): To calculate the chi-square goodness-of-fit test statistic, you need:
A. Observed and expected counts across categories
B. Only the sample mean
C. Only the 20th and 80th percentiles
D. Only the p-value
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: state that the chi-square goodness-of-fit test evaluates whether observed counts match expected counts from a specified distribution
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Chi-Square Goodness-Of-Fit Test): The chi-square goodness-of-fit test evaluates whether sample data fit a specified distribution by _____ across categories.
**Answer**: comparing observed and expected counts

#### LO 3: identify the chi-square distribution as the reference distribution for the goodness-of-fit test
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Chi-Square Distribution): The _____ is the reference distribution used to conduct the goodness-of-fit hypothesis test.
**Answer**: chi-square distribution

#### LO 4: explain why the chi-square distribution is appropriate for modeling the goodness-of-fit test statistic
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Distribution): Which distribution serves as the reference for modeling the goodness-of-fit test statistic?
A. Chi-Square Distribution
B. Uniform Distribution
C. Exponential Distribution
D. Percentiles And Quartiles
**Answer**: A

#### LO 5: use the chi-square distribution to obtain a p-value from a computed test statistic
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Distribution): After computing the test statistic, which distribution do you use to obtain the p-value for this test?
A. Chi-Square Distribution
B. Uniform Distribution
C. Exponential Distribution
D. Null Hypothesis (H0)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: state the null hypothesis that the data follow the specified uniform or exponential distribution
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Null Hypothesis (H0)): The null hypothesis (H0) is the statement that the data _____ the specified distribution.
**Answer**: follow the specified distribution

#### LO 7: state the alternative hypothesis that the data do not follow the specified distribution
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Alternative Hypothesis (Ha)): The alternative hypothesis (Ha) is the statement that the data _____ the specified distribution.
**Answer**: do not follow the specified distribution

#### LO 8: calculate the observed frequency in each category by tallying sample receipts in the defined intervals
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Observed Frequency): Observed frequency refers to:
A. The count of sample observations in each category
B. The count predicted for each category under the hypothesized distribution
C. The area under the distribution corresponding to the test statistic
D. The computed value used with the chi-square distribution
**Answer**: A

#### LO 9: calculate the expected frequency for each category under the hypothesized uniform or exponential distribution
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Expected Frequency): Expected frequency for a category under the hypothesized distribution is:
A. The count predicted for each category under the hypothesized distribution
B. The count of sample observations in each category
C. The final judgment based on the p-value
D. The exponential rate parameter 1/x̄
**Answer**: A

#### LO 10: calculate expected counts for all categories and determine whether each meets the minimum expected count of five
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Expected Frequency Requirement): Each category must have an expected count of at least _____; categories may be combined to meet this condition.
**Answer**: five

#### LO 11: recognize that each category must have an expected count of at least five and that categories may be combined to meet this condition
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Expected Frequency Requirement): If some categories have small expected counts, the requirement is satisfied by ensuring each has at least:
A. Five expected counts, possibly by combining categories
B. Five observed counts in each category
C. Ten expected counts in total across all categories
D. Equal observed and expected counts in each category
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: calculate the 20th, 40th, 60th, and 80th percentiles for X ~ U(lowest value, highest value) to define five equal-probability categories
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Uniform Distribution): For X ~ U(lowest value, highest value), which cut points define five equal-probability categories?
A. 20th, 40th, 60th, and 80th percentiles
B. Quartiles only (25th, 50th, 75th)
C. Sample mean (x̄) only
D. 20th and 80th percentiles only
**Answer**: A

#### LO 13: state the uniform model to be tested as X ~ U(lowest value, highest value) based on the sample’s minimum and maximum
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Uniform Distribution): State the uniform model to be tested: X ~ _____.
**Answer**: U(lowest value, highest value)

#### LO 14: calculate the required percentile cut points and expected counts for X ~ Exp(1/x̄) using the sample mean
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Exponential Distribution): For the exponential model used in testing, which parameterization and input are used to obtain cut points and expected counts?
A. X ~ Exp(1/x̄) using the sample mean
B. X ~ U(lowest value, highest value) using quartiles
C. Chi-Square Distribution using the test statistic
D. Percentiles and quartiles without a model
**Answer**: A

#### LO 15: calculate the exponential decay parameter as 1/x̄ from the sample data
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Decay Parameter (Exponential)): The exponential decay parameter is specified as _____.
**Answer**: 1/x̄

#### LO 16: construct category intervals using the specified percentiles for the hypothesized uniform or exponential distribution
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Cells (Categories)): In this context, 'cells' are best described as:
A. Intervals defined to tally observed and expected counts for the test
B. The area under the distribution corresponding to the test statistic
C. The sample average used to set parameters
D. The final judgment based on the p-value
**Answer**: A

#### LO 17: categorize each observation into the appropriate cell to produce observed tallies
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Cells (Categories)): Placing each observation into its appropriate cell primarily produces:
A. Observed tallies per interval
B. The p-value
C. The decay parameter 1/x̄
D. The null hypothesis statement
**Answer**: A

#### LO 18: calculate the specified quartiles and percentiles used to define category boundaries for the tests
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Percentiles And Quartiles): Percentiles and quartiles in this setting are used to:
A. Define category boundaries
B. Compute the p-value
C. State the null hypothesis
D. Determine the decay parameter
**Answer**: A

#### LO 19: categorize data values into intervals according to the calculated percentiles and quartiles
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Percentiles And Quartiles): After calculating percentiles and quartiles, you should next:
A. Categorize data values into intervals according to them
B. Compute the sample mean only
C. Decide to reject H0
D. Calculate the decay parameter 1/x̄
**Answer**: A

#### LO 20: calculate the chi-square test statistic from the observed and expected frequencies across categories
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Test Statistic): Which quantity is computed from observed and expected frequencies across categories and used with the chi-square distribution to perform the test?
A. Test statistic
B. P-value
C. Observed frequency
D. Expected frequency
**Answer**: A

#### LO 21: calculate the p-value corresponding to the chi-square test statistic using the chi-square distribution
**Multiple Choice** (concept: P-Value): The p-value corresponds to:
A. The area under the chi-square distribution associated with the test statistic
B. The count of observations per category
C. The hypothesized model X ~ U(lowest value, highest value)
D. The sample average x̄
**Answer**: A

#### LO 22: interpret the p-value in context to decide whether the sample provides evidence against the specified distribution
**Multiple Choice** (concept: P-Value): Interpreting the p-value in context is used to decide whether the sample provides:
A. Evidence against the specified distribution
B. The exact percentile cut points
C. The sample's minimum and maximum
D. The decay parameter 1/x̄
**Answer**: A

#### LO 23: state the decision to reject or fail to reject the null hypothesis based on the p-value
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Decision And Conclusion): The decision and conclusion provide the _____ based on the p-value and a complete-sentence summary of the result.
**Answer**: final judgment

#### LO 24: summarize the conclusion in a complete sentence about whether the receipts fit the uniform or exponential distribution
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Decision And Conclusion): Which component summarizes in a complete sentence whether the receipts fit the uniform or exponential distribution?
A. Decision And Conclusion
B. Null Hypothesis (H0)
C. Test Statistic
D. P-Value
**Answer**: A

#### LO 25: calculate the sample mean of receipt amounts to parameterize the exponential model
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sample Mean (x̄)): The sample mean (x̄) is the _____ used to set the exponential decay parameter.
**Answer**: sample average


---
## Section: seg_155

#### LO 1: State when to use the chi-square test of independence and its right-tailed nature and cell-count requirement.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Test Of Independence): When is the chi-square test of independence appropriate, and what are its tail and cell-count requirements?
A. When comparing two categorical factors; it is right-tailed and requires adequate expected cell counts.
B. When comparing means of two groups; it is two-tailed and has no cell-count requirement.
C. When predicting a numeric outcome; it is left-tailed and requires observed counts to be at least five.
D. When testing one proportion; it is right-tailed and requires equal row totals.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: Calculate the chi-square test of independence results from a contingency table by comparing observed to expected frequencies to obtain the test statistic and p-value.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Test Of Independence): Which procedure correctly calculates results for a chi-square test of independence from a contingency table?
A. Compute expected counts as (row total)(column total)/total surveyed, then compute Σ(O − E)²/E, and obtain a right-tail p-value from the chi-square distribution.
B. Compute sample means for each factor, take the difference, and use a two-tailed normal distribution.
C. Use observed counts only to get ΣO and compare to a left-tail probability from the chi-square distribution.
D. Compute expected counts as (row total + column total)/total surveyed, then take Σ|O − E| and use a two-tailed chi-square distribution.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: State the null hypothesis that the two categorical factors are independent.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Null Hypothesis (Independence Test)): In a chi-square test of independence, the null hypothesis states that the two factors are ____.
**Answer**: independent

#### LO 4: Define a contingency table as a display of sample values for two factors used to assess possible dependence and conditional probabilities.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Contingency Table): A contingency table is a table that ____ for two different factors and facilitates determining conditional probabilities.
**Answer**: displays sample values

#### LO 5: Construct a contingency table from categorized sample data on two factors.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Contingency Table): Which step correctly constructs a contingency table from categorized data on two factors?
A. Place counts into cells for each combination of factor categories and include row and column totals.
B. Plot the data on a scatterplot with a best-fit line.
C. Compute the mean of each factor and compare them directly.
D. Randomly assign cases to groups to balance the table.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: Interpret a contingency table to discuss potential dependence between two categorical factors.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Contingency Table): When interpreting a contingency table, which use is appropriate?
A. Discuss potential dependence by comparing cell patterns and determine conditional probabilities.
B. Prove dependence whenever any cell count is large.
C. Establish causation between the two factors.
D. Analyze only numeric variables with the table.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 7: Define the degrees of freedom for a chi-square test of independence as (i − 1)(j − 1).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom (Independence)): For a chi-square test of independence, the degrees of freedom are df = ____ , where i is the number of rows and j is the number of columns.
**Answer**: (i − 1)(j − 1)

#### LO 8: Calculate the degrees of freedom for a chi-square test of independence from the numbers of rows and columns in a contingency table.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom (Independence)): A contingency table has 3 rows and 4 columns. What is the degrees of freedom for the chi-square test of independence?
A. 7
B. 6
C. 12
D. 3
**Answer**: B

#### LO 9: Calculate the expected cell count using E = (row total)(column total) / total surveyed.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Expected Cell Count): If a cell's row total is 20, column total is 30, and the total surveyed is 100, what is the expected cell count?
A. 6
B. 20
C. 30
D. 50
**Answer**: A

#### LO 10: State the formula for an expected cell count in a contingency table.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Expected Cell Count): The expected cell count in a contingency table is E = ____.
**Answer**: (row total)(column total) / total surveyed

#### LO 11: Define the expected cell frequency condition as requiring each expected value to be at least five.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Expected Cell Frequency Condition): The expected cell frequency condition requires each expected value to be ____.
**Answer**: at least five

#### LO 12: Calculate expected cell frequencies and check that each is at least five before conducting the test.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Expected Cell Frequency Condition): Expected counts are [4.8, 5.2, 6.0, 3.9]. What should you conclude about the expected cell frequency condition?
A. It is not satisfied; some expected values are less than five.
B. It is satisfied because the average expected value exceeds five.
C. It is satisfied because at least one expected value is five or more.
D. It does not apply to chi-square tests of independence.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 13: Calculate the chi-square test statistic for independence using Σ (O − E)² / E from observed and expected frequencies.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Chi-Square Test Statistic (Independence)): Using Σ (O − E)² / E, what is the chi-square contribution from two cells with (O, E) = (12, 10) and (8, 10)?
A. 0.8
B. 0.4
C. 1.6
D. 2.0
**Answer**: A

#### LO 14: Define the chi-square test statistic for independence as Σ (O − E)² / E.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Chi-Square Test Statistic (Independence)): The chi-square test statistic for independence is ____ , where O are observed values and E are expected values.
**Answer**: Σ (O − E)² / E

#### LO 15: Identify the chi-square test of independence as a right-tailed test.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Right-Tailed Test (Independence)): The chi-square test of independence is ____.
**Answer**: right-tailed

#### LO 16: Explain why the chi-square test of independence is right-tailed, noting that the nonnegative statistic grows with greater discrepancy between observed and expected counts.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Right-Tailed Test (Independence)): Why is the chi-square test of independence right-tailed?
A. The nonnegative statistic increases with greater discrepancy between observed and expected counts, so large values in the right tail indicate dependence.
B. Small negative values indicate stronger dependence in the left tail.
C. Because it compares sample means, both tails are used.
D. Because the degrees of freedom are always one.
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_159

#### LO 1: identify whether a given equation is linear by checking if it can be written as y = a + bx
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Linear Equation): Which equation is linear because it can be written as y = a + bx?
A. y = 3 + 2x
B. y = 2x^2 + 1
C. y = 4/x + 5
D. x = 5
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: formulate a linear equation y = a + bx from a verbal description involving a fixed amount and a per-unit rate
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Linear Equation): A cost is a fixed amount of 10 plus 3 per unit x. Which equation models this as y = a + bx?
A. y = 10 + 3x
B. y = 3 + 10x
C. y = 10x + 3x
D. y = 10 - 3x
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: interpret a linear equation by explaining how y depends on x in the relationship y = a + bx
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Linear Equation): For y = 4 + 3x, which statement correctly explains how y depends on x?
A. y is 4 more than 3 times x
B. y is 3 more than 4 times x
C. y is 4 times x plus 3
D. y is 3 plus x/4
**Answer**: A

#### LO 4: define the independent variable x in y = a + bx as the value chosen to solve for y
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Independent Variable): In y = a + bx, the independent variable is ______ for which a value is chosen to solve the equation.
**Answer**: the variable x

#### LO 5: describe the role of the independent variable x in determining the dependent variable y
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Independent Variable): Which statement best describes the role of x in y = a + bx?
A. x is chosen first, and then y is solved using y = a + bx
B. y is chosen first, and then x is solved
C. x is the output after choosing y
D. x and y are both fixed constants
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: define the dependent variable y in y = a + bx as the output solved for after choosing x
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Dependent Variable): In y = a + bx, the dependent variable is ______ that is solved for after choosing a value of x.
**Answer**: the variable y

#### LO 7: describe how the dependent variable y changes in response to chosen values of x
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Dependent Variable): Which statement describes how y changes in y = a + bx?
A. After choosing x, y is computed from y = a + bx
B. y is chosen first, and then x is computed
C. y stays constant regardless of x
D. y determines the value of a and b
**Answer**: A

#### LO 8: describe the graph of y = a + bx as a straight line and note that any non-vertical line can be represented by such an equation
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Graph Of A Linear Equation): The graph of y = a + bx is a ______.
**Answer**: straight line

#### LO 9: construct the graph of a linear equation y = a + bx
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Graph Of A Linear Equation): Which pair of points lies on the graph of y = 1 + 2x?
A. (0, 1) and (2, 5)
B. (0, 2) and (2, 5)
C. (1, 1) and (2, 3)
D. (0, 1) and (1, 2)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 10: define the slope b in y = a + bx as the number that describes the line’s steepness
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Slope): For y = a + bx, b is a number that describes the ______ of the line.
**Answer**: steepness of the line

#### LO 11: interpret the slope b as the rate of change in a given context
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Slope): In a relationship modeled by y = a + bx, each increase of 1 in x changes y by 3. What is b?
A. 3
B. 0
C. -3
D. 1/3
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: define the y-intercept a in y = a + bx as the y-coordinate of the point where the line crosses the y-axis
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Y-Intercept): For y = a + bx, a is the ______ of the point (0, a) where the line crosses the y-axis.
**Answer**: y-coordinate

#### LO 13: interpret the y-intercept a as the initial or fixed amount when x = 0 in context
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Y-Intercept): For y = 7 + 2x, which statement correctly interprets a?
A. When x = 0, y = 7; 7 is the initial or fixed amount
B. When x = 0, y = 2; 2 is the initial or fixed amount
C. 2 is the steepness of the line and the initial amount
D. 7 is how much y changes per unit increase in x
**Answer**: A

#### LO 14: identify from the sign of b whether the line rises (b > 0), is horizontal (b = 0), or falls (b < 0)
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Slope Sign Interpretation): If b < 0, the line slopes ______ to the right.
**Answer**: downward

#### LO 15: interpret the sign of b to explain the direction the line slopes on its graph
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Slope Sign Interpretation): For y = a + bx with b = 0, which statement is correct?
A. The line is horizontal
B. The line slopes upward to the right
C. The line slopes downward to the left
D. The line is vertical
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_161

#### LO 1: construct a scatter plot from paired (x, y) data using appropriate technology settings (lists, stat plot, and ZoomStat).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Scatter Plot): From paired (x, y) data, which display shows their relationship?
A. Scatter plot
B. Separate lists of x and y without plotting
C. A single horizontal line through the mean of y
D. A regression line computed without plotting
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: interpret a scatter plot to infer the apparent relationship between two variables, including direction, strength, and general form.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Scatter Plot): When interpreting a scatter plot, what are you primarily inferring?
A. The relationship between two variables x and y
B. The distribution of x alone
C. Only exact numeric values of each point
D. The specific regression equation regardless of pattern
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: identify whether high values of one variable occur with high or low values of the other in a scatter plot.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Direction Of A Relationship): In a scatter plot, the direction of a relationship indicates whether high values of one variable occur with high (or low) values of the other; a clear direction is ____.
**Answer**: high-high/low-low or high-low

#### LO 4: describe what constitutes a clear direction in a scatter plot, such as high-high or low-low versus high-low pairings.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Direction Of A Relationship): Which best describes a clear direction in a scatter plot?
A. High-high/low-low or high-low pairings
B. No discernible pattern at all
C. All points on a horizontal line with no relationship
D. Exact equality of x and y at every point
**Answer**: A

#### LO 5: describe the strength of a relationship by how closely scatter plot points cluster around a line or other functional pattern, with closer alignment indicating stronger association.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Strength Of A Relationship): The strength of a relationship is determined by how close the points are to a ____; for a linear relationship, it is stronger when points are close to a straight line.
**Answer**: line or other function

#### LO 6: recognize that a scatter plot with all points on a horizontal line indicates no relationship between the variables.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Horizontal Line Indicates No Relationship): A scatter plot where all points fall on a ______ shows no relationship, despite a perfect fit.
**Answer**: horizontal line

#### LO 7: interpret a perfect-fit horizontal scatter pattern as showing no relationship despite all points lying on a line.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Horizontal Line Indicates No Relationship): A perfect horizontal line formed by scatter plot points indicates:
A. No relationship between the variables
B. A strong positive linear relationship
C. A strong negative linear relationship
D. A strong predictive relationship suitable for regression
**Answer**: A

#### LO 8: examine a scatter plot to note the overall pattern and any deviations from that pattern.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Overall Pattern And Deviations): When examining a scatter plot, focus on the ______ and any deviations from that pattern.
**Answer**: overall pattern

#### LO 9: identify deviations from the overall pattern in a scatter plot.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Overall Pattern And Deviations): In addition to the overall pattern in a scatter plot, what should you look for?
A. Deviations from that pattern
B. Exact values of every point
C. Only the mean of y
D. Only whether x equals y
**Answer**: A

#### LO 10: identify when a scatter plot suggests a linear pattern and whether the points are close to a straight line.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Linear Relationship): Which scatter pattern suggests a linear relationship?
A. Points following an approximate straight-line pattern
B. Points forming a perfect horizontal line
C. Points with no discernible alignment
D. Points clustered by x with no relation to y
**Answer**: A

#### LO 11: describe the characteristics of a linear relationship in a scatter plot, noting that it is stronger when points lie close to a straight line (excluding horizontal lines).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Linear Relationship): A linear relationship occurs when points follow an approximate straight-line pattern and is stronger when points are ______ (excluding horizontal lines).
**Answer**: close to a straight line

#### LO 12: state the condition for calculating a regression line: only when one variable helps to explain or predict the other, with x treated as independent and y as dependent for prediction.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Condition For Using A Regression Line): Only calculate a regression line ______.
**Answer**: if one variable helps to explain or predict the other

#### LO 13: explain why a regression line should be used only when an explanatory–predictive relationship exists between variables and how it supports predicting y from x.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Condition For Using A Regression Line): When is it appropriate to use a regression line?
A. Only when one variable helps to explain or predict the other
B. Whenever points lie on any line, including horizontal
C. Any time two variables are measured, regardless of relationship
D. When you cannot see any overall pattern
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_163

#### LO 1: Calculate the least-squares regression line for paired data by determining a and b that minimize SSE.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Line Of Best Fit (Least-Squares Regression Line)): How is the least-squares regression line obtained for paired data?
A. Choose a and b that minimize the sum of squared residuals.
B. Choose a and b that minimize the sum of residuals.
C. Choose a and b that maximize the correlation r.
D. Choose a and b so the line passes through all points.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: Calculate the regression equation ŷ = a + bx using b = Σ(x − x̄)(y − ȳ)/Σ(x − x̄)² and a = ȳ − b x̄.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Regression Equation): Given x̄ = 2, ȳ = 5, Σ(x − x̄)(y − ȳ) = 12, and Σ(x − x̄)² = 3, what is the regression equation?
A. ŷ = −3 + 4x
B. ŷ = 5 + 4x
C. ŷ = −3 + 2x
D. ŷ = 5 + 2x
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: Interpret the regression equation by relating b to the average change in y per unit x and a to the y-value where the line crosses the y-axis.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Regression Equation): Which statement correctly interprets a and b in ŷ = a + bx?
A. b is the average change in y per unit x; a is where the line crosses the y-axis.
B. b is the predicted y when x = 0; a is the average change in y per unit x.
C. a and b are both correlation measures.
D. a is the slope; b is the y-intercept.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 4: Define ŷ as the estimated value of y from the regression line for a given x, generally not equal to the observed y.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Predicted Value (Ŷ)): Fill in the blank: ŷ is the _____ obtained from the regression line for a given x; generally not equal to the observed y.
**Answer**: estimated value of y

#### LO 5: Calculate ŷ for a specified x using the regression equation.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Predicted Value (Ŷ)): Given ŷ = 2 + 3x, what is ŷ when x = 4?
A. 12
B. 14
C. 8
D. 5
**Answer**: B

#### LO 6: Define the residual ε as y − ŷ, the vertical distance between an observed y and its predicted value.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Residual (Error)): Fill in the blank: The residual is ε = _____; the vertical distance between an observed y and its predicted value.
**Answer**: y − ŷ

#### LO 7: Calculate residuals for data points by computing y − ŷ and determine their signs.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Residual (Error)): For a data point with y = 10 and ŷ = 8, what is the residual and its sign?
A. ε = 2, positive
B. ε = −2, negative
C. ε = 2, negative
D. ε = −2, positive
**Answer**: A

#### LO 8: Define SSE as the sum of squared residuals, Σ ε².
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sum Of Squared Errors (SSE)): Fill in the blank: SSE is the sum of squared residuals, ____.
**Answer**: Σε²

#### LO 9: Calculate SSE from a set of residuals by summing their squares.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sum Of Squared Errors (SSE)): Given residuals ε = {1, −2, 3}, what is SSE?
A. 2
B. 6
C. 14
D. 0
**Answer**: C

#### LO 10: Define the least squares criterion as choosing a and b that minimize SSE among all possible lines.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Least Squares Criterion): Fill in the blank: The least squares criterion chooses a and b that _____ among all possible lines.
**Answer**: minimize SSE

#### LO 11: Calculate b using Σ(x − x̄)(y − ȳ)/Σ(x − x̄)² or b = r(sy/sx).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Slope (b)): If r = 0.5, sy = 4, and sx = 2, what is b using b = r(sy/sx)?
A. 1
B. 2
C. 0.25
D. −1
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: Define the y-intercept a as the value where the regression line crosses the y-axis (predicted y when x = 0).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Y-Intercept (a)): Fill in the blank: The y-intercept a is the value where the regression line _____.
**Answer**: crosses the y-axis

#### LO 13: Calculate a using a = ȳ − b x̄.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Y-Intercept (a)): If x̄ = 3, ȳ = 5, and b = 2, what is a using a = ȳ − b x̄?
A. −1
B. 1
C. 11
D. −11
**Answer**: A

#### LO 14: Interpret linear regression as fitting the least-squares best-fit line under the assumption that data are scattered about a straight line.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Linear Regression): Which best describes linear regression?
A. Fitting the least-squares best-fit line assuming data are scattered about a straight line.
B. Fitting a curve that passes through every point regardless of SSE.
C. Choosing the line with the largest slope.
D. Using nonlinear models to predict y.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 15: Define r as a measure between −1 and +1 of the strength and direction of the linear association between x and y, with sign matching the slope.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Correlation Coefficient (r)): Fill in the blank: r is a measure between _____ of the strength and direction of the linear association between x and y; its sign matches the slope.
**Answer**: −1 and +1

#### LO 16: Define r² as the square of r representing the proportion of variation in y explained by x using the regression line.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Coefficient Of Determination (r²)): Fill in the blank: r² is the _____ of r representing the proportion of variation in y explained by x using the regression line.
**Answer**: square

#### LO 17: Interpret r² and 1 − r² as the explained and unexplained percentages of variation in y in context.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Coefficient Of Determination (r²)): If r² = 0.64, which is correct?
A. About 64% of the variation in y is explained by x; about 36% is unexplained.
B. About 36% of the variation in y is explained by x; about 64% is unexplained.
C. All variation is explained because r is positive.
D. None of the variation is explained unless r = ±1.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 18: State that the least-squares regression line always passes through (x̄, ȳ).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Best-Fit Line Passes Through (X̄, Ȳ)): Fill in the blank: The least-squares regression line always passes through (_____).
**Answer**: x̄, ȳ

#### LO 19: Use the regression line to predict y for x-values within the observed domain of the sample data.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Prediction Within Sample Domain): Observed x-values range from 10 to 50. Is predicting y at x = 40 appropriate?
A. Yes, because 40 is within the observed domain.
B. Yes, because linear models allow any extrapolation.
C. No, because predictions are never appropriate.
D. No, unless r = 1.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 20: Explain why predictions for x-values outside the sample domain may not be appropriate.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Prediction Within Sample Domain): Why might predicting y at x far outside the observed domain be inappropriate?
A. The linear relationship may not hold beyond the sample’s x-range.
B. SSE cannot be computed for new x-values.
C. Residuals must be zero outside the domain.
D. Correlation r becomes undefined outside the domain.
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_165

#### LO 1: define the correlation coefficient r as the sample statistic that measures the strength and direction of the linear relationship between x and y and serves as an estimate of ρ
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Correlation Coefficient (r)): The correlation coefficient r is a ______ that measures the strength and direction of the linear relationship between x and y.
**Answer**: sample statistic

#### LO 2: interpret r by describing its sign and magnitude in terms of the direction and strength of the sample’s linear relationship, recognizing that sample size affects its significance
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Correlation Coefficient (r)): Which statement best interprets r?
A. Its sign shows the direction and its magnitude shows the strength of the sample’s linear relationship; significance depends on n.
B. Its magnitude shows the direction and its sign shows the strength; significance does not depend on n.
C. It measures any association (linear or not) and does not relate to ρ.
D. It is a population parameter fixed at 0 or 1.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: define ρ as the unknown population correlation coefficient that quantifies the linear association between x and y in the population
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Population Correlation Coefficient (ρ)): The population correlation coefficient ρ is the ______ describing the linear association in the population.
**Answer**: unknown population parameter

#### LO 4: interpret ρ relative to zero as indicating whether a linear relationship exists in the population, acknowledging that r from the sample is used to test whether ρ ≠ 0
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Population Correlation Coefficient (ρ)): What does ρ = 0 indicate?
A. No linear relationship in the population.
B. r must be zero in every sample.
C. A significant linear relationship in the population.
D. That α must be set to 0.05.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 5: state the hypotheses H0: ρ = 0 and Ha: ρ ≠ 0 and the two equivalent decision frameworks (p-value or critical values)
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Significance Test For Correlation Coefficient): Which pair of hypotheses and decision frameworks is correct for testing correlation?
A. H0: ρ = 0, Ha: ρ ≠ 0; decide by p-value or by critical values.
B. H0: r = 0, Ha: r ≠ 0; decide only by p-value.
C. H0: ρ ≥ 0, Ha: ρ < 0; decide only by critical values.
D. H0: r ≥ 0, Ha: r < 0; decide by α only.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: calculate, given r and n, the test statistic and p-value (or compare r to critical values) to decide whether ρ is significantly different from zero
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Significance Test For Correlation Coefficient): Given r and n, which steps lead to a decision about whether ρ differs from zero?
A. Compute t and its two-tailed p-value (df = n − 2) and compare to α, or compare r to critical values.
B. Compute z with df = n − 1 and compare to α only.
C. Compare r to ±1; if not ±1, reject H0.
D. Compute t but ignore sample size when deciding.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 7: describe in words that under H0 the population correlation is not significantly different from zero and there is no significant linear relationship between x and y
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Null Hypothesis (H0: ρ = 0)): Under H0, the population correlation is ______ and there is no significant linear relationship.
**Answer**: not significantly different from zero

#### LO 8: state Ha: ρ ≠ 0
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Alternative Hypothesis (Ha: ρ ≠ 0)): Which symbolic alternative hypothesis is used?
A. Ha: ρ ≠ 0
B. Ha: r ≠ 0
C. Ha: ρ = 0
D. Ha: r = 0
**Answer**: A

#### LO 9: describe in words that under Ha the population correlation is significantly different from zero and there is a significant linear relationship between x and y
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Alternative Hypothesis (Ha: ρ ≠ 0)): Under Ha, the population correlation is ______ and a significant linear relationship exists.
**Answer**: significantly different from zero

#### LO 10: define the p-value method for testing H0: ρ = 0 versus Ha: ρ ≠ 0 as rejecting H0 when the two-tailed p-value from a t-distribution with n − 2 degrees of freedom is less than α
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: P-Value Method): In the p-value method, reject H0 when the p-value from a t-distribution (df = n − 2) is ______.
**Answer**: less than α

#### LO 11: compare r to the positive and negative critical values for df = n − 2 at α = 0.05 to determine whether r is significant
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Critical Values Method): By the critical values method, r is significant when it is:
A. outside the interval between the negative and positive critical values (df = n − 2 at α = 0.05).
B. between −1 and 1 regardless of df.
C. equal to the critical value of t.
D. greater than α.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: compute t = r√(n − 2) / √(1 − r²) for a given r and n
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Test Statistic (t) For Correlation): Which formula gives the test statistic for correlation?
A. t = r√(n − 2) / √(1 − r²)
B. t = r√(n) / √(1 − r)
C. t = r²(n − 2)
D. t = √(1 − r²) / r
**Answer**: A

#### LO 13: calculate the degrees of freedom for the test as df = n − 2
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom): For the correlation test, the degrees of freedom are df = ______.
**Answer**: n − 2

#### LO 14: calculate whether r is significant using the p-value or critical value method and conclude that a significant linear relationship exists and the regression line can be used for prediction within the observed x-domain when the scatter plot is linear
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Significant Correlation Coefficient): When r is significant and the scatter plot is linear, what conclusion is appropriate?
A. A significant linear relationship exists; use the regression line for modeling and prediction within the observed x-domain.
B. No linear relationship exists; avoid any use of the regression line.
C. A non-linear relationship exists; extrapolation is recommended.
D. The regression line may be used beyond the observed x-domain.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 15: list the conditions for prediction: r is significant, the scatter plot shows a linear trend, and predictions are made only within the observed x-domain
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Conditions For Using The Regression Line For Prediction): One key condition for using the regression line for prediction is: ______.
**Answer**: do not extrapolate beyond the observed x-domain

#### LO 16: define the independence assumption as residual errors being mutually independent with no pattern
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Independence Of Residuals): The independence assumption states that residual errors are ______.
**Answer**: mutually independent


---
## Section: seg_167

#### LO 1: Calculate a predicted mean response by substituting a specified x within the observed x range into the least-squares regression equation.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Prediction Using Least-Squares Regression): Given y-hat = 1 + 2x and observed x range 0 to 10, what is the predicted mean response at x = 4?
A. 9
B. 8
C. 12
D. 4
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: Identify when the least-squares regression line can be used for prediction based on whether the specified x lies within the observed x range.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Prediction Using Least-Squares Regression): Observed x range is 5 to 20. Can the least-squares regression line be used to predict at x = 22?
A. Yes, because 22 is close to the range.
B. Yes, the regression line can always be used.
C. No, because 22 lies outside the observed x range.
D. No, because regression lines predict x from y.
**Answer**: C

#### LO 3: Calculate a predicted y-value from a given least-squares regression line for a specified x within the observed x domain.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Least-Squares Regression Line): Given the least-squares regression line y-hat = -3 + 0.5x and observed x domain 0 to 10, what is y-hat at x = 6?
A. 0
B. 3
C. -3
D. 6
**Answer**: A

#### LO 4: Differentiate predictions made with the least-squares regression line as interpolation versus extrapolation.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Least-Squares Regression Line): Observed x values range from 100 to 200. Which x corresponds to interpolation when using the least-squares regression line?
A. 90
B. 150
C. 210
D. 250
**Answer**: B

#### LO 5: Identify the domain of observed x-values for a dataset used in regression.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Domain Of Observed X Values): Observed x-values are 2, 4, 6, 7, 9. Which interval is the domain of observed x-values?
A. 2 to 9
B. 4 to 7
C. 0 to 10
D. 2 to 6
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: Classify a specified x-value as inside or outside the domain of observed x-values.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Domain Of Observed X Values): Observed x-values are 3, 5, 6, and 8. Is x = 8 inside or outside the domain of observed x-values?
A. Inside, because it is within the observed range (3 to 8).
B. Outside, because it is an endpoint.
C. Outside, because it is not the mean.
D. Inside, because any x is allowed.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 7: Define interpolation as predicting within the observed x-values of the data.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Interpolation): Interpolation is predicting ____.
**Answer**: inside of the observed x values in the data.

#### LO 8: Calculate an interpolated prediction by substituting an x-value within the observed range into the regression equation.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Interpolation): Given y-hat = 10 + x and observed x range 0 to 100, what is the interpolated prediction at x = 40?
A. 50
B. 40
C. 10
D. 140
**Answer**: A

#### LO 9: Define extrapolation as predicting outside the observed x-values of the data.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Extrapolation): Extrapolation is predicting ____.
**Answer**: outside of the observed x values in the data.

#### LO 10: Classify a proposed prediction as extrapolation when the specified x-value lies outside the observed x range.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Extrapolation): Observed x range is 1 to 5. A prediction is proposed at x = 6. How should this prediction be classified?
A. Interpolation, because it is inside the range.
B. Extrapolation, because it lies outside the observed x range.
C. Interpolation, because it is close to 5.
D. Neither, because the regression line is not usable.
**Answer**: B
