## Section: seg_169

#### LO 1: identify potential outliers in a scatterplot by using residuals and the ±2s guideline around the least squares line.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Outliers): Which approach flags potential outliers using residuals and the ±2s guideline around the least squares line?
A. Compute each residual and flag points with |residual| > 2s or that fall outside lines drawn at ±2s parallel to the best-fit line.
B. Flag any points with large x-values regardless of residual size.
C. Flag points with residuals smaller than s.
D. Flag points closest to the line of best fit.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: calculate residual magnitudes and the 2s cutoff to determine which points qualify as potential outliers.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Outliers): After computing |residual| and 2s, which rule determines potential outliers?
A. |residual| > 2s
B. residual > 2s (ignore negative residuals)
C. |residual| > s
D. residual < 2s
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: examine flagged outliers to decide whether they arise from data errors or contain meaningful information and whether to exclude or retain them.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Outliers): After points are flagged by the ±2s guideline, what should you do next?
A. Investigate causes, correct or delete erroneous values, retain informative ones, and document decisions.
B. Automatically delete all flagged points.
C. Automatically keep all flagged points.
D. Modify the regression line to pass through all flagged points.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 4: define a residual as the vertical distance y − ŷ from an observed point to the line of best fit.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Residual (Error)): A residual is the ______ from a data point to the line of best fit, computed as observed y minus predicted y (y − ŷ).
**Answer**: vertical distance

#### LO 5: calculate residuals for each observation using the regression equation’s predicted values.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Residual (Error)): How do you compute a residual for an observation?
A. Observed y minus predicted y (y − ŷ) using the regression’s predicted value.
B. Predicted y minus observed y (ŷ − y).
C. Observed y minus the average of y-values.
D. The vertical distance from the origin to the point.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: define an influential point as an observation far in the x-direction that can substantially change the regression slope.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Influential Points): An influential point is a data point far from others in the ______ that can substantially affect the slope of the regression line.
**Answer**: horizontal (x) direction

#### LO 7: differentiate outliers from influential points by focusing on large vertical residuals versus extreme horizontal positions that alter slope.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Influential Points): Which statement correctly distinguishes outliers from influential points?
A. Outliers have large vertical residuals; influential points are far in x and can change the slope.
B. Both are defined solely by large vertical residuals.
C. Outliers are far in x; influential points are far in y.
D. Influential points always have small residuals by definition.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 8: calculate s from SSE and sample size using n − 2 degrees of freedom.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Standard Deviation Of Residuals (s)): Which description matches how s is obtained?
A. Compute s from SSE using n − 2 degrees of freedom.
B. Compute s as the average residual.
C. Compute s from SSE using n degrees of freedom.
D. Compute s as the slope of the best-fit line.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 9: identify s in regression or calculator output (e.g., LinRegTTest) for use in outlier detection.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Standard Deviation Of Residuals (s)): Which reported value should you use as s for outlier thresholds?
A. The standard deviation of the residuals (s).
B. The intercept of the line (b0).
C. The correlation (r).
D. The sum of squared errors (SSE).
**Answer**: A

#### LO 10: define SSE as the sum of squared residuals across all data points.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sum Of Squared Errors (SSE)): SSE is the ______ across all data points.
**Answer**: sum of the squared residuals

#### LO 11: calculate SSE by squaring each residual and summing the results.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sum Of Squared Errors (SSE)): How do you compute SSE?
A. Square each residual and sum the results.
B. Sum the residuals without squaring.
C. Multiply each residual by s and sum.
D. Square each x-value and sum.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: explain how SSE is used to compute s and how a smaller SSE indicates a line that better fits the data.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sum Of Squared Errors (SSE)): Which statement best describes SSE’s role in fitting?
A. SSE is used to compute s, and a smaller SSE indicates a line that better fits the data.
B. SSE is unrelated to s, and a larger SSE indicates a better fit.
C. SSE equals s exactly.
D. SSE is the slope of the regression line.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 13: calculate the outlier cutoff 2s from the standard deviation of the residuals.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Two-Standard-Deviation Rule For Outliers): Given s, what cutoff is used to flag potential outliers?
A. 2s
B. s²
C. s/2
D. 3s
**Answer**: A

#### LO 14: compare each residual’s magnitude to 2s to decide if the point is a potential outlier.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Two-Standard-Deviation Rule For Outliers): Which comparison applies the two-standard-deviation rule?
A. Flag if |residual| > 2s.
B. Flag if residual > 2s only.
C. Flag if |residual| > s.
D. Flag if |residual| < 2s.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 15: identify data points with |y − ŷ| ≥ 2s as potential outliers.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Two-Standard-Deviation Rule For Outliers): Under the two-standard-deviation rule, points with |y − ŷ| ≥ ______ are potential outliers.
**Answer**: 2s

#### LO 16: identify potential outliers by graphing lines parallel to the best-fit line at ±2s and seeing which points fall outside them.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Graphical Identification Of Outliers): To flag potential outliers graphically, draw lines parallel to the best-fit line at ______; points outside these lines are flagged.
**Answer**: ±2s

#### LO 17: calculate equations for the ±2s lines (same slope as the best-fit line with intercept shifted by ±2s) to support graphical outlier detection.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Graphical Identification Of Outliers): If the best-fit line is y = b0 + b1x, which equations represent the ±2s lines?
A. y = (b0 ± 2s) + b1x
B. y = b0 + (b1 ± 2s)x
C. y = b0 + b1x ± 2sx
D. y = (b0 ± s) + (b1 ± s)x
**Answer**: A

#### LO 18: calculate each residual and the value 2s to support numerical outlier checks.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Numerical Identification Of Outliers): To prepare for a numerical outlier check, what should you compute?
A. Each residual and 2s.
B. Each x-value and 3s.
C. Only s.
D. Only the slope and intercept.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 19: compare computed residuals to ±2s and note which exceed the threshold.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Numerical Identification Of Outliers): How do you flag potential outliers in a numerical check?
A. Mark observations with |residual| > 2s.
B. Mark observations with residual < 0.
C. Mark observations with |residual| < 2s.
D. Mark observations with the largest x-values.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 20: identify which observations are potential outliers based on the numerical |residual| ≥ 2s rule.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Numerical Identification Of Outliers): Numerically, an observation is a potential outlier if |residual| ≥ ______.
**Answer**: 2s

#### LO 21: identify appropriate actions after flagging outliers, including verifying data, correcting errors, deleting erroneous points, retaining informative points, and documenting decisions.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Handling Outliers): Which set of actions is appropriate after flagging outliers?
A. Verify data, correct errors or delete erroneous values, retain informative outliers, and document decisions or report with and without removed data.
B. Delete all outliers immediately.
C. Keep all outliers regardless of cause.
D. Alter data so no outliers remain, without documentation.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 22: calculate a new regression line and correlation coefficient after removing a verified erroneous outlier.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Handling Outliers): After removing a verified erroneous outlier, what should you do?
A. Recompute the regression line and correlation without that point.
B. Keep the original regression and only note the deletion.
C. Set all residuals to zero.
D. Recompute only s, not the line or correlation.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 23: examine how removing an outlier changes the slope, SSE, and correlation, and whether predictions improve.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Handling Outliers): What should you examine after removing an outlier?
A. Changes in slope, SSE, and correlation, and whether predictions improve.
B. Only changes in the intercept.
C. Only whether the correlation stays the same.
D. Only the number of data points remaining.
**Answer**: A

B. The variable designated as independent when analyzing distance versus cost.
C. Paired data on distance an individual lives from school and the cost of supplies for the current term.
D. The line sketched on the graph obtained from the calculator or computer based on the data.

B. The variable designated as dependent when analyzing distance versus cost.

C. The graphed line representing the linear equation for the data.
D. A calculated parameter in the linear equation.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 21: justify whether a detected outlier should be removed or retained and why.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Outlier): In your justification, recall that an outlier is a data point ______.
**Answer**: identified as an outlier in the set


---
## Section: seg_175

#### LO 1: Classify equations and relationships as linear by recognizing the forms y = mx + b and y = a + bx in context.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Linear Equation): Which equation is linear?
A. y = 3 + 2x
B. y = 1 + 2x^2
C. y = 4/x + 1
D. y = 2^x + 3
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: Calculate predicted y values for specified x using a given linear equation in the form y = a + bx.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Linear Equation): Using y = 5 + 3x, what is the predicted y when x = 4?
A. 17
B. 32
C. 12
D. 9
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: Define the independent variable as the x variable used to predict or explain changes in y.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Independent Variable): The independent variable is ____ used to predict or explain changes in the dependent variable.
**Answer**: the variable x

#### LO 4: Explain why a variable should be treated as independent in a study (for example, year or weight).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Independent Variable): When modeling y (fuel efficiency) based on x (vehicle weight), why is weight treated as the independent variable?
A. Because it is the variable x used to predict or explain changes in y.
B. Because it is the variable y whose values depend on x.
C. Because it minimizes the Sum of Squared Errors.
D. Because it is where the line crosses the y-axis.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 5: Calculate the slope b of a linear or regression equation from data or output.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Slope): In the equation y = −1 + 0.5x, what is the slope b?
A. 0.5
B. -1
C. x
D. y
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: Interpret the slope b as the average change in y for each one-unit increase in x within the given context.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Slope): In ŷ = 50 + 2x (x = hours studied, y = test score), what does the slope mean?
A. For each one-unit increase in x, y increases by about 2 points.
B. When x = 0, y is about 2 points.
C. For each extra hour, y increases by about 50 points.
D. It is Pearson’s r.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 7: Define slope as the coefficient b representing the rate of change between x and y in y = a + bx.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Slope): In y = a + bx, the slope is the ____ that represents the rate of change in y for a one-unit increase in x.
**Answer**: coefficient b

#### LO 8: Interpret a scatter plot to assess direction, linearity, and strength of the relationship between x and y.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Scatter Plot): A scatter plot shows points trending upward in a roughly straight, tight pattern as x increases. What does this suggest?
A. A strong, positive, approximately linear relationship.
B. A strong, negative, approximately linear relationship.
C. No relationship.
D. A positive but clearly non-linear relationship.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 9: Define residuals as the differences between observed y values and predicted values ŷ from the regression line.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Residuals): Residuals are the ____ between observed y values and the corresponding estimated y values from the regression line.
**Answer**: differences

#### LO 10: Calculate residuals for given data points using a computed regression line.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Residuals): Given ŷ = 2 + x and the observed point (x = 3, y = 10), what is the residual?
A. 5
B. -5
C. 2
D. 3
**Answer**: A

#### LO 11: Define the Sum of Squared Errors (SSE) as the sum of squared residuals.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sum Of Squared Errors): The Sum of Squared Errors (SSE) is the ____.
**Answer**: sum of squared residuals

#### LO 12: Explain how minimizing SSE determines the least-squares regression line.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sum Of Squared Errors): Why is the least-squares regression line preferred?
A. It minimizes the Sum of Squared Errors, providing a uniform best fit.
B. It maximizes the residuals so outliers are emphasized.
C. It sets Pearson’s r equal to +1.
D. It forces the line through every point.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 13: Interpret r to describe the strength and direction (positive or negative) of the linear association between x and y.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Coefficient Of Correlation): If the correlation coefficient r = −0.85, what does this indicate?
A. A strong negative linear association between x and y.
B. A strong positive linear association between x and y.
C. No linear association between x and y.
D. A perfect negative linear association.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 14: Define the coefficient of determination r² as the proportion of variation in y explained by x via the regression line.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Coefficient Of Determination): The coefficient of determination r² is the ____ of variation in y explained by x via the regression line.
**Answer**: proportion

#### LO 15: Interpret r² as the percent of variability in y accounted for by the linear model.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Coefficient Of Determination): If r² = 0.64, how should this be interpreted?
A. About 64% of the variation in y is explained by x via the regression line.
B. About 36% of the variation in x is explained by y.
C. The correlation r equals 0.64.
D. There is no linear association.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 16: Detect potential outliers by comparing absolute residuals to 2s or by checking points above a + bx + 2s or below a + bx − 2s.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Standard Deviation Of Residuals): For a model ŷ = a + bx with s = 3, a point has ŷ = 20 and observed y = 28. How is this point classified using the 2s rule?
A. Potential outlier above the line because residual > 2s.
B. Not unusual because residual < 2s.
C. Potential outlier below the line because residual > 2s.
D. Cannot tell without r.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 17: State the null and alternative hypotheses for testing the population correlation (for example, H0: ρ = 0 versus Ha: ρ ≠ 0).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Significance Testing Of Correlation Coefficient): Which hypotheses test whether a linear relationship exists in the population?
A. H0: ρ = 0 versus Ha: ρ ≠ 0
B. H0: r² = 1 versus Ha: r² < 1
C. H0: b = 0 versus Ha: b ≠ 0 for sample only
D. H0: μx = μy versus Ha: μx ≠ μy
**Answer**: A

#### LO 18: Use the least-squares regression line to predict y for specified x-values within the observed data range.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Prediction Using Regression): Observed x-values range from 0 to 10. Using ŷ = 2 + 3x, what is the predicted y at x = 8?
A. 26
B. 24
C. 30
D. 34
**Answer**: A

#### LO 19: Define an outlier as an observation that does not fit the pattern of the rest of the data.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Outlier): An outlier is ____ that does not fit the pattern of the rest of the data.
**Answer**: an observation

#### LO 20: Identify predictions as extrapolation when the x-value is outside the range of the observed data.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Extrapolation): If the observed x-range is 2 to 9, a prediction made at x = 12 is best described as:
A. Extrapolation, which should be avoided.
B. Interpolation within the data range.
C. An outlier.
D. A residual.
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_179

#### LO 1: define one-way ANOVA as a test that determines whether there is a statistically significant difference among several group means using variances.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: One-Way ANOVA): One-Way ANOVA is a test used to determine the existence of a ______, using variances to assess whether the means are equal.
**Answer**: statistically significant difference among several group means

#### LO 2: list the five assumptions required to perform a one-way ANOVA: normal populations; random, independent samples; equal population standard deviations (variances); a categorical factor; and a numerical response.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Assumptions Of One-Way ANOVA): Which of the following is NOT an assumption required for a one-way ANOVA?
A. Populations are normal.
B. Samples are randomly selected and independent.
C. Populations have equal standard deviations (or variances).
D. The factor is a numerical variable.
**Answer**: D

#### LO 3: define the null hypothesis for a one-way ANOVA as equality of all group population means (μ1 = μ2 = ... = μk).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Null Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA)): In a one-way ANOVA, the null hypothesis states that all group population means are ______.
**Answer**: equal

#### LO 4: compare the null and alternative hypotheses for a one-way ANOVA in terms of equality versus inequality of group means.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Null Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA)): Which statement correctly compares the null (H0) and alternative (Ha) hypotheses in a one-way ANOVA?
A. H0: at least two means differ; Ha: all means are equal.
B. H0: all group population means are equal; Ha: at least two group population means are not equal.
C. H0: group means are normally distributed; Ha: variances are equal.
D. H0: samples are independent; Ha: samples are dependent.
**Answer**: B

#### LO 5: define the alternative hypothesis for a one-way ANOVA as the statement that at least two group means differ (μi ≠ μj for some i ≠ j).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Alternative Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA)): The alternative hypothesis in a one-way ANOVA states that at least two group population means are ______.
**Answer**: not equal

#### LO 6: contrast the null and alternative hypotheses by highlighting that H0 asserts all group means are equal while Ha asserts that at least two are not equal.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Alternative Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA)): Which choice highlights the key contrast between H0 and Ha in a one-way ANOVA?
A. H0 asserts all group means are equal; Ha asserts at least two are not equal.
B. H0 asserts populations are normal; Ha asserts populations are skewed.
C. H0 asserts samples are independent; Ha asserts samples are random.
D. H0 asserts factors are numerical; Ha asserts responses are categorical.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 7: define a factor as a categorical variable in a one-way ANOVA.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Factor): In one-way ANOVA, a factor is a ______ variable.
**Answer**: categorical

#### LO 8: differentiate the factor from the response by noting that the factor is categorical whereas the response is numerical.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Factor): Which pair correctly differentiates the factor from the response in a one-way ANOVA?
A. Factor: categorical; Response: numerical.
B. Factor: numerical; Response: categorical.
C. Factor: numerical; Response: numerical.
D. Factor: categorical; Response: categorical.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 9: define a response as a numerical variable in a one-way ANOVA.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Response): In one-way ANOVA, the response is a ______ variable.
**Answer**: numerical

#### LO 10: classify variables as factors or responses in a one-way ANOVA context based on whether they are categorical or numerical.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Response): In a one-way ANOVA, how should variables be classified?
A. Factor is categorical; response is numerical.
B. Factor is numerical; response is numerical.
C. Factor is categorical; response is categorical.
D. Factor and response are both categorical or numerical depending on the hypothesis.
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_181

#### LO 1: define the F distribution as a family derived from squared t-values with separate numerator and denominator degrees of freedom and notation F ~ Fdf(num),df(denom).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: F Distribution): Fill in the blank: The F distribution used for F-tests has ______.
**Answer**: separate numerator and denominator degrees of freedom

#### LO 2: describe how the F distribution is parameterized by df(num) and df(denom), is used for F-tests such as ANOVA, and relates to the t-distribution.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: F Distribution): Which statement best describes the F distribution?
A. It is used for F-tests, is parameterized by separate numerator and denominator degrees of freedom, and is derived from squared t-values.
B. It is used for t-tests and has a single degrees of freedom parameter.
C. It is unrelated to the t-distribution and uses left-tailed tests in ANOVA.
D. It is a distribution of raw t-values and is denoted t ~ t(df).
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: calculate the mean of an F distribution using μ = df(denom)/(df(denom) − 2).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: F Distribution): Using μ = df(denom)/(df(denom) − 2), what is the mean of an F distribution (when defined)?
A. df(denom)/(df(denom) − 2)
B. df(num)/(df(num) − 2)
C. df(denom)/(df(denom) + 2)
D. (df(num) + df(denom))/2
**Answer**: A

#### LO 4: define the F statistic as the ratio F = MSbetween/MSwithin.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: F Statistic (F-Ratio)): Fill in: The F statistic is F = ______.
**Answer**: MSbetween/MSwithin

#### LO 5: calculate the F statistic from an ANOVA table as MSbetween divided by MSwithin.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: F Statistic (F-Ratio)): From an ANOVA table, F is calculated as:
A. MSbetween divided by MSwithin
B. SSwithin divided by SSbetween
C. MSwithin divided by MSbetween
D. SSbetween divided by dfwithin
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: interpret an observed F ratio near 1 as consistent with H0 and larger values in the right tail as evidence against H0.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: F Statistic (F-Ratio)): An observed F ratio near 1 is most consistent with which conclusion?
A. Consistent with H0; larger right-tail values are evidence against H0
B. Strong evidence against H0 in a left-tailed test
C. Indicates MSbetween is much larger than MSwithin, so H0 must be false
D. Inconclusive because F can be negative
**Answer**: A

#### LO 7: calculate SS, df, MS, and F for a one-way ANOVA from grouped data or software output using the given formulas.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: One-Way ANOVA): Which set correctly pairs one-way ANOVA components with their formulas?
A. dfbetween = k − 1; dfwithin = n − k; F = MSbetween/MSwithin
B. dfbetween = n − k; dfwithin = k − 1; F = SSbetween/SSwithin
C. dfbetween = k; dfwithin = n; F = MSwithin/MSbetween
D. dfbetween = k − 1; dfwithin = n − 1; F = SSwithin/dfwithin
**Answer**: A

#### LO 8: state that one-way ANOVA compares more than two group means, is preferred over multiple t-tests to limit Type I error, and uses a right-tailed F test.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: One-Way ANOVA): Fill in: In one-way ANOVA, the hypothesis test is always ______.
**Answer**: right-tailed

#### LO 9: define the null hypothesis for one-way ANOVA as all group population means are equal.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Null Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA)): Fill in: The null hypothesis for one-way ANOVA states: ______.
**Answer**: All group population means are equal

#### LO 10: explain that if the null hypothesis is true, MSbetween and MSwithin estimate the same variance and the F ratio is approximately 1.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Null Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA)): If the null hypothesis is true in one-way ANOVA, which is expected?
A. MSbetween and MSwithin estimate the same variance and F is approximately 1
B. MSbetween greatly exceeds MSwithin and F is near 0
C. MSwithin exceeds MSbetween and F is negative
D. Only MSbetween estimates the common variance and F is left-tailed
**Answer**: A

#### LO 11: define the alternative hypothesis for one-way ANOVA as at least two group population means differ (populations have different normal distributions).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Alternative Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA)): Fill in: The alternative hypothesis for one-way ANOVA states: ______.
**Answer**: At least two sample groups come from populations with different normal distributions

#### LO 12: define variance between samples as the variance of the sample means multiplied by n when group sizes are equal (weighted otherwise), also called explained variation.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Variance Between Samples (Between-Groups Variation)): Fill in: Variance between samples is also called ______.
**Answer**: explained variation

#### LO 13: calculate the variance between samples from the variance of group means multiplied by n for equal group sizes or by a weighted approach for unequal sizes.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Variance Between Samples (Between-Groups Variation)): For equal group sizes, the variance between samples is computed as:
A. The variance of the sample means multiplied by n
B. The average of the sample variances
C. SSwithin divided by dfwithin
D. The variance of all individual observations divided by n
**Answer**: A

#### LO 14: describe how MSbetween reflects explained variation and increases when population group means truly differ.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Variance Between Samples (Between-Groups Variation)): Which statement about MSbetween is correct?
A. It reflects explained variation and increases when population group means truly differ.
B. It reflects unexplained variation and is unaffected by differences among group means.
C. It equals SSwithin/dfwithin.
D. It is approximately 1 under the null hypothesis.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 15: define variance within samples as the pooled average of sample variances (weighted when sizes differ), also called unexplained variation.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Variance Within Samples (Within-Groups Variation)): Fill in: Variance within samples is also called ______.
**Answer**: unexplained variation

#### LO 16: calculate the variance within samples as the mean of the sample variances (pooled variance) or as MSwithin = SSwithin/dfwithin.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Variance Within Samples (Within-Groups Variation)): Variance within samples can be calculated as:
A. The mean of the sample variances (pooled variance) or MSwithin = SSwithin/dfwithin
B. The variance of the sample means multiplied by n
C. SSbetween/dfbetween
D. SStotal − SSbetween divided by k − 1
**Answer**: A

#### LO 17: describe that MSwithin estimates the common population variance and is unaffected by differences among group means.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Variance Within Samples (Within-Groups Variation)): Which statement best describes MSwithin?
A. It estimates the common population variance and is unaffected by differences among group means.
B. It measures explained variation and increases when group means differ.
C. It is computed as SSbetween/dfbetween.
D. It equals F when H0 is true.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 18: define SSbetween as the sum of squares representing variation among different samples.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sum Of Squares Between (SSbetween)): Fill in: SSbetween is the sum of squares representing ______.
**Answer**: variation among different samples

#### LO 19: describe how to obtain SSbetween from group sums and sizes using the provided expression.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sum Of Squares Between (SSbetween)): Which expression gives SSbetween from group sums s_j and sizes n_j?
A. Σ[(s_j)²/n_j] − (Σ s_j)²/n
B. Σx² − (Σx)²/n
C. SStotal − SSwithin
D. (Σ s_j)/n
**Answer**: A

#### LO 20: calculate SSbetween using SSbetween = Σ[(s_j)²/n_j] − (Σ s_j)²/n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sum Of Squares Between (SSbetween)): According to the given formula, SSbetween equals:
A. Σ[(s_j)²/n_j] − (Σ s_j)²/n
B. SSwithin + SStotal
C. Σx²/n − (Σx)²
D. (Σ s_j)²/n − Σ[(s_j)²/n_j]
**Answer**: A

#### LO 21: define SSwithin as the sum of squares representing variation within samples due to chance.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sum Of Squares Within (SSwithin)): Fill in: SSwithin is the sum of squares representing ______ due to chance.
**Answer**: variation within samples

#### LO 22: calculate SSwithin as SStotal − SSbetween.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sum Of Squares Within (SSwithin)): How is SSwithin obtained?
A. SSwithin = SStotal − SSbetween
B. SSwithin = SSbetween − SStotal
C. SSwithin = SSbetween + SStotal
D. SSwithin = (Σx)²/n − Σx²
**Answer**: A

#### LO 23: define SStotal as the total sum of squares of all values minus the square of the total sum divided by n.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Total Sum Of Squares (SStotal)): Fill in: SStotal is the ______ of all values combined minus the square of the total sum divided by n.
**Answer**: sum of squares

#### LO 24: calculate SStotal from raw data using SStotal = Σx² − (Σx)²/n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Total Sum Of Squares (SStotal)): Which formula computes SStotal from raw data?
A. SStotal = Σx² − (Σx)²/n
B. SStotal = (Σx)² − Σx²/n
C. SStotal = Σ[(s_j)²/n_j] − (Σ s_j)²/n
D. SStotal = (Σ s_j)²/n
**Answer**: A

#### LO 25: define MSbetween and MSwithin as variance estimates computed by dividing the corresponding sum of squares by its degrees of freedom.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Mean Square (MS)): Fill in: Mean square (MS) is a ______.
**Answer**: variance estimate

#### LO 26: calculate MSbetween = SSbetween/dfbetween and MSwithin = SSwithin/dfwithin.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Mean Square (MS)): Which formulas define MSbetween and MSwithin?
A. MSbetween = SSbetween/dfbetween; MSwithin = SSwithin/dfwithin
B. MSbetween = SSwithin/dfbetween; MSwithin = SSbetween/dfwithin
C. MSbetween = SStotal/dfbetween; MSwithin = SStotal/dfwithin
D. MSbetween = SSbetween × dfbetween; MSwithin = SSwithin × dfwithin
**Answer**: A

#### LO 27: state that dfbetween = k − 1.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom Between (dfbetween)): Fill in: dfbetween = ______.
**Answer**: k − 1

#### LO 28: calculate dfbetween from the number of groups as k − 1.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom Between (dfbetween)): Given k groups, dfbetween equals:
A. k − 1
B. n − k
C. k
D. n − 1
**Answer**: A

#### LO 29: define dfwithin as n − k.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom Within (dfwithin)): Fill in: dfwithin = ______.
**Answer**: n − k

#### LO 30: calculate dfwithin from the total sample size and number of groups as n − k.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom Within (dfwithin)): In a one-way ANOVA with total sample size n and k groups, dfwithin is:
A. n − k
B. k − 1
C. n
D. n − 1
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_183

#### LO 1: state that the F distribution is not symmetrical and is skewed to the right.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: F Distribution Shape): The F distribution is not symmetrical and ____.
**Answer**: skewed to the right

#### LO 2: describe the right-skewed, non-symmetrical shape of the F distribution.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: F Distribution Shape): Which best describes the shape of the F distribution?
A. Symmetrical and bell-shaped
B. Uniform across its range
C. Not symmetrical and skewed to the right
D. Skewed to the left
**Answer**: C

#### LO 3: calculate the appropriate numerator and denominator degrees of freedom to select the correct F distribution curve for a one-way ANOVA.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: F Distribution Degrees Of Freedom): To select the correct F distribution curve for a one-way ANOVA with df(num)=3 and df(denom)=20, which statement is correct?
A. Any F curve can be used; the degrees of freedom do not matter.
B. Use the F curve specific to df(num)=3 and df(denom)=20.
C. Use a two-tailed F curve centered at zero.
D. Use the same curve as a t distribution with df=23.
**Answer**: B

#### LO 4: identify the numerator and denominator degrees of freedom that determine a specific F distribution curve.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: F Distribution Degrees Of Freedom): An F distribution curve is determined by a set of ____ and ____ degrees of freedom.
**Answer**: numerator and denominator

#### LO 5: state the notation Fdf(num),df(denom) for the F distribution.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: F Distribution Notation): The F distribution is denoted as ____, specifying numerator and denominator degrees of freedom.
**Answer**: Fdf(num),df(denom)

#### LO 6: interpret Fdf(num),df(denom) by linking the first degrees of freedom to the numerator and the second to the denominator.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: F Distribution Notation): In the notation Fdf(num),df(denom), what do the two degrees of freedom correspond to?
A. First to denominator, second to numerator
B. Both to the numerator
C. First to the numerator, second to the denominator
D. Both to the denominator
**Answer**: C

#### LO 7: calculate df(num) = k − 1 and df(denom) = n − k for a given number of groups k and total sample size n.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom In One-Way ANOVA F-Test): In a one-way ANOVA with k = 5 groups and total sample size n = 30, what are df(num) and df(denom)?
A. df(num) = 5, df(denom) = 30
B. df(num) = 4, df(denom) = 25
C. df(num) = 4, df(denom) = 30
D. df(num) = 5, df(denom) = 25
**Answer**: B

#### LO 8: state that in one-way ANOVA, df(num) = k − 1 and df(denom) = n − k.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom In One-Way ANOVA F-Test): In one-way ANOVA, df(num) = ____ and df(denom) = ____.
**Answer**: k − 1 and n − k

#### LO 9: define the F statistic as the ratio of the between-group mean square (MSbetween) to the within-group mean square (MSwithin).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: F Statistic (F Ratio)): The F statistic in one-way ANOVA is the ____.
**Answer**: ratio of the between-group mean square to the within-group mean square

#### LO 10: calculate the F statistic from an ANOVA table using MSbetween and MSwithin.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: F Statistic (F Ratio)): An ANOVA table reports MSbetween = 12 and MSwithin = 3. What is the F statistic?
A. 0.25
B. 4
C. 36
D. 15
**Answer**: B

#### LO 11: calculate the p-value for an F test as P(F > observed F) using the right tail of the F distribution with the appropriate degrees of freedom.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: P-Value For F Test): For an F test with observed F = 5.2, which expression correctly gives the p-value using the F distribution?
A. P(F < 5.2) using the left tail
B. P(|F| > 5.2) in both tails
C. P(F > 5.2) using the right tail
D. 1 − P(F > 5.2) using the right tail
**Answer**: C

#### LO 12: state that the p-value for an F test is the right-tail probability P(F > observed F).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: P-Value For F Test): The p-value for an F test is computed as ____ using the right tail of the F distribution.
**Answer**: P(F > observed F)


---
## Section: seg_185

#### LO 1: Compare two sample variances using the F ratio to assess evidence for or against equal population variances.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: F Test Of Two Variances): Which statement best describes the F test of two variances?
A. A procedure using the F distribution to compare two population variances.
B. A procedure using the F distribution to compare two population means.
C. A procedure using the F distribution to test a single variance.
D. A procedure using the F distribution to compare two sample means.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 2: State that the F test of two variances requires independent populations that are normally distributed.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Assumptions For Two-Variance F Test): For the two-variance F test, the two populations must be ____ and independent.
**Answer**: normally distributed

#### LO 3: Define the F statistic for two variances as F = (s1^2/σ1^2) / (s2^2/σ2^2) and, under H0: σ1^2 = σ2^2, as F = s1^2 / s2^2.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: F Statistic For Two Variances): Under H0: σ1^2 = σ2^2, the F statistic for two variances reduces to F = ____.
**Answer**: s1^2 / s2^2

#### LO 4: Calculate F from sample variances as s1^2/s2^2 when testing σ1^2 = σ2^2.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: F Statistic For Two Variances): When testing H0: σ1^2 = σ2^2, how is F computed from sample variances s1^2 and s2^2?
A. s1^2 / s2^2
B. s1 / s2
C. s1^2 + s2^2
D. s1^2 − s2^2
**Answer**: A

#### LO 5: State that the test statistic follows F ~ F(n1 − 1, n2 − 1) with numerator degrees of freedom n1 − 1 and denominator degrees of freedom n2 − 1.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: F Distribution And Degrees Of Freedom): In the two-variance F test, the test statistic follows ____ with numerator df n1 − 1 and denominator df n2 − 1.
**Answer**: F(n1 − 1, n2 − 1)

#### LO 6: Calculate the numerator and denominator degrees of freedom for an F test given sample sizes n1 and n2.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: F Distribution And Degrees Of Freedom): For n1 = 12 and n2 = 18, what are the numerator and denominator degrees of freedom for the F test?
A. (12, 18)
B. (11, 17)
C. (11, 18)
D. (12, 17)
**Answer**: B

#### LO 7: State the null hypothesis σ1^2 = σ2^2 and the possible alternatives σ1^2 < σ2^2, σ1^2 > σ2^2, or σ1^2 ≠ σ2^2.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Hypotheses And Tail Direction): In the F test of two variances, the null hypothesis is ____.
**Answer**: σ1^2 = σ2^2

#### LO 8: Classify an F test as left-tailed, right-tailed, or two-tailed based on the form of the alternative hypothesis.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Hypotheses And Tail Direction): If the alternative is σ1^2 < σ2^2, the F test is:
A. Left-tailed
B. Right-tailed
C. Two-tailed
D. Not applicable
**Answer**: A

#### LO 9: Explain how the orientation of the F ratio depends on the alternative hypothesis and why placing the larger sample variance in the numerator yields F ≥ 1.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Orientation Of F Ratio): Placing the larger sample variance in the numerator makes the F ratio:
A. Always less than 1
B. Equal to zero
C. Greater than or equal to 1
D. Negative
**Answer**: C

#### LO 10: Calculate the correctly oriented F ratio (s1^2/s2^2 or s2^2/s1^2) for given sample variances and a stated alternative.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Orientation Of F Ratio): Given s1^2 = 5 and s2^2 = 12 with alternative σ2^2 > σ1^2, which F ratio should be used?
A. s1^2 / s2^2
B. s2^2 / s1^2
C. s1^2 − s2^2
D. s1 / s2
**Answer**: B

#### LO 11: Interpret F values close to one as supporting equal variances and much larger F (with the larger sample variance in the numerator) as evidence against equality.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Interpretation Of F Magnitude): Which interpretation aligns with the F test of two variances?
A. F close to one supports equal variances; a much larger F (with the larger sample variance in the numerator) indicates evidence against equality.
B. F close to zero supports equal variances; larger F supports equality.
C. Any F greater than one proves variances are equal.
D. F close to one implies population means are equal.
**Answer**: A

#### LO 12: State that the F test for two variances is highly sensitive to non-normality and may yield misleading p-values.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sensitivity To Non-Normality): The F test for two variances is very sensitive to deviations from ____ and can yield unpredictable p-values when distributions are not normal.
**Answer**: normality

#### LO 13: Describe how non-normal population distributions can produce p-values that are unpredictably too high or too low in the F test of two variances.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sensitivity To Non-Normality): When population distributions are not normal, the F test of two variances may produce p-values that are:
A. Consistently too high
B. Consistently too low
C. Unpredictably too high or too low
D. Unaffected by distribution shape
**Answer**: C


---
## Section: seg_187

#### LO 1: state the purpose of ANOVA and the conditions under which it is applicable (normality, equal variances, random and independent sampling).
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Analysis Of Variance (ANOVA)): ANOVA tests whether the _____ are equal under normality, equal standard deviations, and random, independent sampling; the test statistic is the F-ratio.
**Answer**: means of three or more populations

#### LO 2: compute ANOVA components (SSbetween, SSwithin, MSbetween, MSwithin) and the resulting F-ratio for grouped data.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Analysis Of Variance (ANOVA)): Which statistic is used in ANOVA to compare variation among group means to variation within groups?
A. F-ratio (F statistic)
B. t statistic
C. chi-square statistic
D. z-score
**Answer**: A

#### LO 3: justify the decision to reject or not reject the null hypothesis by comparing the ANOVA p-value to a stated significance level.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Analysis Of Variance (ANOVA)): An ANOVA yields p-value = 0.03 at alpha = 0.05. What is the decision?
A. Reject H0 because p < alpha
B. Fail to reject H0 because p < alpha
C. Reject H0 because p > alpha
D. Fail to reject H0 because p > alpha
**Answer**: A

#### LO 4: state the null and alternative hypotheses for a one-way ANOVA comparing three or more group means.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: One-Way ANOVA): Which pair states the hypotheses for a one-way ANOVA?
A. H0: all group means are equal; Ha: at least one group mean differs
B. H0: all variances are equal; Ha: at least one variance differs
C. H0: the population is normal; Ha: the population is not normal
D. H0: all group means differ; Ha: all group means are equal
**Answer**: A

#### LO 5: compute group means, sample variances, degrees of freedom, mean squares, and the F statistic for a one-way ANOVA.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: One-Way ANOVA): Which conditions must hold to properly apply a one-way ANOVA?
A. Normal populations, random and independent samples, and equal standard deviations
B. Two populations only and nonnormal data
C. Nonrandom, dependent sampling is allowed
D. Test statistic is t with n−1 degrees of freedom
**Answer**: A

#### LO 6: interpret the F statistic and p-value to conclude whether at least one group mean differs from the others.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: One-Way ANOVA): If a one-way ANOVA yields a large F and a small p-value, what is the conclusion?
A. At least one group mean differs from the others
B. All group means are equal
C. All group variances are equal
D. Sample sizes must be equal
**Answer**: A

#### LO 7: define variance as the mean of the squared deviations from the mean and as the square of the standard deviation.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Variance): Variance is the _____.
**Answer**: mean of the squared deviations from the mean

#### LO 8: calculate a sample variance from raw data using the sum of squared deviations divided by n minus 1.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Variance): Which formula defines the sample variance?
A. Sum of squared deviations divided by n − 1
B. Sum of deviations divided by n
C. Square root of the mean of squared deviations
D. Mean of absolute deviations
**Answer**: A

#### LO 9: describe the F distribution as positive and right-skewed with numerator and denominator degrees of freedom.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: F Distribution): The F distribution is always _____ and skewed right.
**Answer**: positive

#### LO 10: state the F distribution and its two degrees of freedom used for the ANOVA test statistic.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: F Distribution): The ANOVA test statistic follows which distribution and degrees of freedom structure?
A. F distribution with a single df of n − 1
B. F distribution with numerator and denominator degrees of freedom
C. t distribution with df = k − 1
D. No degrees of freedom are needed
**Answer**: B

#### LO 11: define the F statistic as the ratio of between-group variation to within-group variation.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: F Statistic (F-Ratio)): The F statistic is the ratio of _____.
**Answer**: between-group variation to within-group variation

#### LO 12: calculate the F statistic as MSbetween divided by MSwithin (or using balanced-design shortcuts when applicable).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: F Statistic (F-Ratio)): How is the F statistic calculated in one-way ANOVA?
A. MSbetween / MSwithin
B. MSwithin / MSbetween
C. SSbetween / SSwithin
D. SStotal / SSwithin
**Answer**: A

#### LO 13: interpret the magnitude of an F statistic in terms of evidence against equal means (large F implies small p-value; small F implies large p-value).
**Multiple Choice** (concept: F Statistic (F-Ratio)): Which interpretation of F is correct?
A. A larger F implies a smaller p-value and more evidence against equal means
B. A larger F implies a larger p-value and less evidence against equal means
C. A smaller F implies a smaller p-value
D. The magnitude of F is unrelated to the p-value
**Answer**: A

#### LO 14: list the assumptions for one-way ANOVA: normal populations, random and independent samples, and equal variances.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Assumptions For One-Way ANOVA): One assumption for one-way ANOVA is that populations have _____.
**Answer**: equal standard deviations (or variances)

#### LO 15: state H0 that all group means are equal and Ha that at least one group mean differs.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Hypotheses In One-Way ANOVA): In one-way ANOVA, H0 states all group means are equal; Ha states that _____.
**Answer**: one or more group means differ

#### LO 16: compute df for the F distribution as df(num) = k − 1 and df(denom) = n − k for a one-way ANOVA.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom (F Distribution)): For one-way ANOVA with k groups and n observations, which degrees of freedom are correct?
A. df(num) = k − 1; df(denom) = n − k
B. df(num) = n − k; df(denom) = k − 1
C. df(num) = n − 1; df(denom) = n − k
D. df(num) = k; df(denom) = n − 1
**Answer**: A

#### LO 17: state the numerator and denominator degrees of freedom associated with a given one-way ANOVA design.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom (F Distribution)): The numerator degrees of freedom in a one-way ANOVA equal the _____.
**Answer**: number of groups minus one

#### LO 18: describe how the degrees of freedom in the F distribution relate to the number of groups and the total number of observations.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Degrees Of Freedom (F Distribution)): In one-way ANOVA, the denominator degrees of freedom equal:
A. number of observations minus the number of groups
B. number of groups minus one
C. number of observations minus one
D. number of groups
**Answer**: A

#### LO 19: identify SSbetween as the measure of variation among group means in an ANOVA table.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sum Of Squares Between (SSbetween)): SSbetween measures variation _____.
**Answer**: among group means

#### LO 20: compute SSwithin as SStotal minus SSbetween or by summing within-group sums of squares.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Sum Of Squares Within (SSwithin)): Which relationship is correct for SSwithin?
A. SSwithin = SStotal − SSbetween
B. SSwithin = SSbetween − SStotal
C. SSwithin = SSbetween + SStotal
D. SSwithin = SStotal
**Answer**: A

#### LO 21: state that SSwithin measures variation within groups in one-way ANOVA.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Sum Of Squares Within (SSwithin)): SSwithin measures variation _____.
**Answer**: within groups

#### LO 22: compute SStotal across all observations using a computational formula or deviations from the grand mean.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Total Sum Of Squares (SStotal)): SStotal is the _____.
**Answer**: total variation across all observations

#### LO 23: compute MSbetween by dividing SSbetween by its corresponding degrees of freedom.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Mean Square Between (MSbetween)): MSbetween equals:
A. SSbetween divided by its degrees of freedom
B. SSwithin divided by its degrees of freedom
C. SStotal divided by n
D. SSbetween + SSwithin
**Answer**: A

#### LO 24: define MSwithin as SSwithin divided by its corresponding degrees of freedom.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Mean Square Within (MSwithin)): MSwithin is the _____.
**Answer**: within-groups sum of squares divided by its degrees of freedom

#### LO 25: compute MSwithin from SSwithin and its degrees of freedom.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Mean Square Within (MSwithin)): MSwithin equals:
A. SSwithin / df(within)
B. SSbetween / df(between)
C. SSwithin / df(between)
D. SSbetween / df(within)
**Answer**: A

#### LO 26: state the hypotheses, assumptions, and degrees of freedom for an F test comparing two population variances.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Test Of Two Variances): Which statement correctly specifies the F test for comparing two variances?
A. Uses the F distribution with df equal to n − 1 only; assumes normality and independence
B. Uses the F distribution with numerator df = n1 − 1 and denominator df = n2 − 1; assumes normal distributions and independence between populations
C. Uses the t distribution with df = n1 + n2 − 2; assumes equal variances
D. Uses the chi-square distribution with k − 1 df; no assumptions needed
**Answer**: B

#### LO 27: define pooled variance as the mean of the sample variances across groups.
**Fill-in-the-blank** (concept: Pooled Variance): Pooled variance is the _____.
**Answer**: mean of the sample variances

#### LO 28: calculate the pooled variance from given group sample variances.
**Multiple Choice** (concept: Pooled Variance): How is pooled variance computed from group sample variances?
A. Take the mean of the sample variances
B. Sum the sample variances
C. Take the square root of the sum of sample variances
D. Use the largest sample variance only
**Answer**: A
